LaserOS Wicked Lasers / Laserdock Free cross-platform software for LaserCube, with timeline and ILDA/SVG import no date given maker164

How are lasers controlled inside a bigger production?#

Pangolin describes four ways to run a live show: from a PC or touchscreen; from a MIDI or DMX controller driving the PC; from a lighting console driving the PC; and from a lighting console alone, with content saved onto FB4 hardware. It names grandMA, ChamSys, Hog and Avolites desks, and says the last route is popular with lighting designers.165

On that route DMX mainly triggers stored laser content, and Art-Net is DMX carried over a network (the maker's glossary).129 An FB4 presents a 39-channel DMX profile and ships with more than 3,500 cues on its SD card. From the desk a programmer adjusts size, position, rotation, effects, color and geometric correction (maker's figures, undated).166 Going the other way, BEYOND can appear to the console as a fixture. A 16-channel profile lets the desk drive it much as a MIDI controller would, and in DMX Server mode each projection zone becomes a fixture of 16, 32 or 50 channels, a setup the manual associates with rigs of more than 40 projectors.167 Art-Net and sACN are both supported, on a separate network by the manual's advice, because Art-Net traffic can interfere with FB4 data.167 The same glossary lists OSC as a networking protocol for show control.129

On Beartooth's 2024 tour, eight X-Laser Skywriter fixtures in 5 W and 10 W versions were programmed by the lighting designer as part of a timecoded show on a grandMA2.168 "The true advantage is being able to integrate lasers with standard lights," that designer, Ben Jarrett, told TPi.168 How the desk, the video and the clock fit together around the lasers is the subject of Showfile.

What is the difference between busking and a time-coded show?#

Busking is running cues live to the music. A time-coded show plays back a programmed timeline locked to a clock signal that lights, video and audio also follow.169170

For a timeline show, the music is loaded into the software, effects are laid against the waveform, and the saved show is then triggered from a PC, MIDI, DMX, a lighting console or SMPTE timecode.171 BEYOND accepts MIDI timecode, SMPTE through a reader, Art-Net timecode and Synchronorm, one type at a time. An armed show waits with a flashing "TC" and stops after 1 second without signal unless it has been set to keep running.170 A$AP Rocky's Don't Be Dumb Tour used timeline programming to tie every laser effect to the video content, lighting and music, by Pangolin's account.172 Designer Corbin Sharpe gave EDM Identity the aim in 2026: "I want the lasers to look like they're being controlled by the music."173

The main stage at VELD Festival 2026 was busked: 12 projectors of 40 W each, with built-in FB4s, ran from one BEYOND workstation and no timecode, with cues previsualized in Depence R4 (vendor case study).169 "For busking and live operation, I like to reserve lasers for big moments," said the operator quoted there, identified only as Danny of DC Lasers.169 Live sync aids include tap BPM, automatic BPM detection from an audio input, and DJ-software plugins that report tempo and track position.174

Where does programming meet safety?#

In the zone settings. A projection zone is a named output area with its own geometry. BEYOND handles what its manual calls a virtually unlimited number of them from up to 40 projectors per computer, and content addressed to a zone name that does not exist is blocked.175 Geometric correction warps the output to fit the target, with sliders or a mesh that steps from 1x1 to 9x9.175 The Beam Attenuation Map is a 64 by 64 grid laid over a zone, each cell set from 0 to 100%, used to block or dim output in parts of the picture. The manual's example is keeping beams off the sky or windows.175 Pangolin calls BAM patented and standard in QuickShow, BEYOND and LD2000, and says its PASS product (Professional Audience Scanning Safety System) is considered a standard for audience scanning in the US. Both descriptions are the maker's own.176

Software is one layer of several. Laser safety officer Roberta McHatton, writing for the Event Safety Alliance in 2024, treats zoning as an engineering control, with the operator still watching the beams for hazards such as balloons or crowd surfing after the zones are locked.177 ILDA's one-page safety overview asks for a plan to validate zones before the show.178 Exposure limits are in the safety chapter, and which authority enforces what is in rules.

How do you map lasers onto a building, a mountain or a dome?#

Laser mapping, in Pangolin's definition, is an effect that accentuates specific features of an object, such as tracing a building's edges.179 ILDA gives it an awards category of its own, A01 in the 2026 rules, for shows where outlines fitted to buildings or 3D objects are the main focus.180

The mountain is Stone Mountain, Georgia, where a Lasershow began in 1983. Three high-powered projectors treated the mountain face as a screen about 400 ft square, and because the carved relief varies in depth by about 50 ft, calibration mattered (reported, undated).139 A later upgrade added video projection mapping, with six Barco FLM HD20 projectors laying a "virtual skin" over the granite from three Axon media servers and a Road Hog Full Boar console.181 The classic show ended its 40-year run in spring 2023, replaced by one with lasers, fireworks and drones.182

Steam came early as a screen. "Icarus", in summer 1978, put laser images on steam screens over Washington DC.39 Domes keep their own awards category, and entries must be filmed inside one.180

Who does what on a show day?#

ILDA's safety overview sets the terms: "All laser light shows must be under the direct and personal control of a trained, competent operator." That operator has to see every beam path or use a spotter.178

Titles blur. A laserist was the player of an analog console, and the word survives for anyone who performs a show.81 Modern credits split the work between designers or programmers, who build the show, and operators, who run it.183 The operator is usually also the on-site laser safety officer (LSO).177 ILDA's overview has that person inspecting and testing shutters, interlocks and emergency stops before use, keeping written procedures and a checklist, and briefing crew and performers on where the beams will be.178 McHatton calls setup the riskiest phase, because riggers and stagehands are moving through beam paths, and describes alignment and zoning done at the lowest practical power.177 During the show the emergency stop stays within reach, and anything that went wrong goes into the checklist afterward.178

Whether an LSO is a legal requirement depends on the country. In Germany it is law: the OStrV regulation, section 5(2), has the employer appoint one in writing for Class 3R, 3B or 4 lasers unless the employer has the expertise. In the US the ANSI Z136.1 standard calls for a designated LSO for Class 3B and 4, while FDA variance letters, which set conditions, mention LSOs only for audience scanning. The UK has no formal LSO qualification (ILDA's 2025 summary).18 The UK regulator's guidance asks for competent operators, appropriate supervision, emergency stop functions and handover arrangements between installer and operator.184

Crew size tracks rig size. One country tour credited a designer/programmer, an operator and a technician; one festival deployment used more than 430 lasers with a pyro and special-effects team of 49 (reported, 2023).183 The A$AP Rocky tour credits list a laser operator/programmer, two laser designers, an SFX crew chief and four SFX technicians.172

How long does it take to program a laser show?#

The rule of thumb that circulates comes from a single thread on Pangolin's user forum, dated October 25, 2011. One user gave about 4 hours per program minute for beam shows and about 8 hours per minute for graphics shows, for seasoned programmers. A Pangolin staff member in the same thread reckoned one to two hours of work for 15 seconds of show.185 On those rates a 3-minute time-coded beam show comes to somewhere between 12 and 24 hours and a 3-minute graphics show to about 24 (estimated; arithmetic from the quoted rates). At tour scale, Sharpe reported roughly three to four weeks programming one tour's laser show (2026, reported).173

How do people learn to run laser shows?#

Mostly by standing next to someone who already does. Sharpe, 24 when interviewed, credits shadowing an established operator in 2020 and 2021, then building a home studio of four 5 W lasers and recording timecode shows from July 2023.173 The analog era worked the same way. Laserium's chief laserist Danny Neilson started in 1986, learned under the founder and a producer, and had to win approval before playing public shows.83 The choreography came first and the improvising was earned: "You can't jam all the time," he told MEL Magazine in 2016.83

Formal courses are short. ILDA's 2025 listing has its own "Lasershow Operator" course at 4.75 hours with a test and "Laser Safety Officer for Lasershows" at about 9 hours, alongside ER Productions' two-day "Safe Working with Display Lasers" and Laserworld Academy LSO training.18

What are the ILDA Awards?#

The trade's own prizes, running since 1988. Artistic categories award first, second and third place. Since 2018, ILDA members have judged entry videos online, three judges to a category; before that a three-judge panel did it.186 The Career Achievement Award is the highest honor, voted by members since about 2012, and went ungiven between 1994 and 2003.186

The 2026 rules list 20 artistic categories, among them Laser Mapping, Planetarium Show, Graphics Show, Abstract Show, three Beams/Atmospherics categories, Fine Art Laser Display and a new Sporting Show. Videos run 4 minutes or less, with up to three entries per category, and the prizes are presented at the 2026 ILDA Conference in Salzburg, Austria.180 The Live "Laser Jockey" Performance category is played in person at the conference and judged by the people in the room.180 Technical work gets the Fenning Technical Achievement Awards, which exclude concepts and basic research and include an IDN Standards category open to non-members.180

Cue 07

Why lasers can hurt you, and how shows are made safe

The short version: A visible laser beam passes through the front of the eye and is focused onto the retina, so a few milliwatts in the wrong place can do damage that a far brighter lamp cannot. The exposure limit for a visible beam assumes a blink or a turned head within a quarter of a second, and it works out to about 2.5 mW/cm2. Show safety is the work of keeping every beam a person could meet under that limit - with distance, a wider beam, a moving beam and a fast cutoff when something fails.

How does a laser beam injure an eye?#

Visible and near-infrared light, roughly 380 to 1,400 nm, goes straight through the cornea and lens and is focused on the retina, which is where the damage happens.187 The medical literature names three mechanisms - thermal (cooking), photochemical and ablative - and severity climbs with exposure time, energy and short pulses.187

A lamp throws light in every direction, so an eye across the room collects a sliver of it and images the lamp as a small patch. A laser beam is narrow and close to parallel. If it is thinner than a pupil, the whole beam can enter the eye, and the eye's own lens concentrates it onto a point on the retina. The optics that let a person read small print are the hazard.

What does a quarter of a second have to do with it?#

The limit for a visible continuous beam is built on a reflex. It assumes a person hit by bright light blinks or turns away within 0.25 seconds, the aversion response.188

Maximum Permissible Exposure (MPE) is the highest level of laser light a standard treats as acceptable for an eye or skin over a given time.189 For a visible beam and that quarter second it is about 2.5 mW/cm2. The number comes from a widely used laser safety reference site, since the standards that define it, ANSI Z136.1 and IEC 60825-1, are sold rather than posted.188 Calculators show 2.54 or 2.55 mW/cm2 because of small differences in constants, and the FAA's air traffic order rounds up to 2.6 mW/cm2, so three versions of one limit are in circulation.188190 A 1998 UK doctoral thesis gives it in energy terms, 6.36 J/m2 for an accidental 0.25 s exposure, and says the visible-light value had not changed since the British standard of 1983.191

Longer looks get a lower ceiling: the same reference lists 1.80 mW/cm2 at 1 second and 1.01 mW/cm2 at 10 seconds, against 5.69 mW/cm2 at a hundredth of a second.188

The International Laser Display Association (ILDA), the trade body, says the limits are essentially the same worldwide. What differs between countries is enforcement.189

How far away is far enough?#

Irradiance is power spread over area, and it falls with distance because every beam widens as it travels. Divergence is how fast it widens. The Nominal Ocular Hazard Distance (NOHD) is the point where the beam has spread enough for its irradiance to drop to the MPE, and it depends on two things only: power and divergence.188

The arithmetic is lopsided. Doubling the divergence halves the hazard distance. Cutting the power 100 times shortens it only 10 times, because the relationship follows a square root.188 Widening the beam shortens the hazard distance faster than weakening it does.

Why does it matter that the beam is moving?#

A scanned beam crosses any one eye for a very short time. If the scanner slows or stops, the same light becomes a parked beam. The FDA's 1985 compliance guide, as the O'Hagan thesis summarizes it, said scanned beams with a peak power above 5 mW pose an acute risk of injury if the scan slows or stops, and treated the safeguard against that as a critical feature.191

That safeguard is written into US law. Under 21 CFR 1040.10(f)(9), a laser product that emits scanned radiation must have a scanning safeguard, so that a scan failure cannot expose people above the applicable limits.6

What do the laser classes mean?#

Two systems are in use. The international one is the standard IEC 60825-1, adopted as BS EN 60825-1 in the UK, DIN EN 60825-1 in Germany, JIS C 6802 in Japan and CAN/CSA E60825-1 in Canada.29192193 The US one is law: 21 CFR 1040.10(b) defines each class by the emission-limit table a product's accessible radiation exceeds.6 The FDA publishes its own mapping between the two.194

IEC 60825-1 (standard) US, 21 CFR 1040.10 (law) Output power What the class means
Class 1 Class I - Safe in normal use, long direct viewing included; covers fully enclosed high-power lasers
Class 1M Class I - Safe for the naked eye, can harm through magnifying optics
Class 1C none in the FDA's mapping - Skin-contact medical and cosmetic devices, with eye exposure prevented by engineering
Class 2 Class IIa, II - Visible only (400-700 nm); safe for brief exposure because people turn from bright light; hazardous if stared into
Class 2M Class IIa, II - As Class 2 with a large beam; harmful through magnifiers
Class 3R Class IIIa up to 5 mW Can injure with direct viewing; risk generally low, rising with time
Class 3B Class IIIb up to 500 mW Hazardous even for brief accidental direct exposure; diffuse reflections normally safe
Class 4 Class IV above 500 mW Hazardous to eyes and skin, often a fire hazard

The IEC descriptions are the UK Health Security Agency's, updated in March 2025.29 The US rule counts Class IIIa visible output across 400 to 710 nm.28

Show projectors live at the bottom of that table. The FDA lists "laser light show projectors" as examples of both Class IIIb and Class IV, and the UK's Health and Safety Executive (HSE) puts display lasers at about 200 mW to 40 W or more.19430 ILDA's 2022 draft standard draws the visible line at 5 mW to 499.9 mW for Class 3B and 500 mW and up for Class 4.195

Class also sets the hardware. In the US, 21 CFR 1040.10(f) requires a protective housing and safety interlocks, from Class II up an emission indicator and a beam attenuator, for Class IIIb and IV a remote interlock connector and a key control, and, for Class IV systems made after August 20, 1986, a manual reset.6

What can a Class 4 beam do besides hurt eyes?#

The FDA describes Class IIIb as an immediate skin hazard from the direct beam and an immediate eye hazard when viewed directly. For Class IV it is blunter: "Immediate skin hazard and eye hazard from exposure to either the direct or reflected beam", with a possible fire hazard.194 Reflections count.

HSE says display lasers are a significant eye-injury risk, above about 500 mW they may also burn skin and start fires, and outdoors they can dazzle drivers and pilots.30 The FDA's public summary lists the same four - eye injury, skin burns, fire, and distracting or temporarily blinding pilots and drivers.28

The beam is not the whole risk. A database of 330 laser incidents from 1964 to 1996, compiled by Rockwell and cited by O'Hagan, recorded 24 fires and 12 electric shocks, five of them fatal.196

What is audience scanning?#

Audience scanning is beams deliberately swept through the crowd, at levels meant to stay within the exposure limit. A sheet or tunnel of light passes through the people watching instead of over their heads.

In the US the default answer is no. The standard conditions printed on the FDA's variance application say scanning, projection or reflection into audiences or accessible uncontrolled areas "will not be permitted except for diffuse reflections produced by the atmosphere", by added scattering media and by target screens, and the same form keeps laser light above Class I limits at least 3.0 meters above and 2.5 meters to the side of or below anywhere the public may be.197 These are conditions attached to an approval, binding on whoever holds it. The form does list "Audience scanning" among the effects an applicant can declare, which is the route to a specific approval, and the FDA excludes audience scanning variances from its streamlined renewal process.197198

Germany shows the other approach. The exposure limit is the same, and the question the statutory accident insurers' guidance asks is whether that limit holds in the audience area for the whole show. A beam in the audience space is not banned outright; it may be treated as safe only if it is attenuated, widened, split or moved fast enough that the limits are not exceeded.199 ILDA's country list adds permit regimes on top of that test in Sweden, Switzerland and Austria, though ILDA labels the list unofficial.200

What is ILDA's position on audience scanning?#

ILDA supports the MPE and defends the effect. Its own estimate is that over about three decades some 110 billion laser pulses have entered audience eyes with "essentially no injuries" from continuous-wave shows.189 It follows that with a warning: "Just because a show is legal, or is not stopped, does not necessarily mean that it is safe."189

Its recommended ceilings, measured as a static beam at the closest audience position, are 2.5 mW/cm2 for static beams and 10 mW/cm2 for continuously scanned beams, the second being ILDA's reading of the single-pulse limit. It has also proposed a "Level 2" of up to 100 mW/cm2 under special conditions, and says plainly that this is its own proposal and not law anywhere it knows of.189 ILDA members affirm that pulsed lasers should never be used for audience scanning,200 and since the 2017 ILDA Awards, entries with human exposure must include measurements taken at the time.189

Not everyone has been persuaded. O'Hagan's 1998 thesis, the deepest free study of UK show-laser risk, concluded that audience scanning was "not acceptable in its current form" and that assessing a scanned display is complex.196 ILDA's own draft baseline standard for ordinary shows, Category A, prohibits audience scanning, lighting up people, unterminated beams, pulsed lasers and bounce mirrors, to keep the category easy to check.195

How is a beam kept within the limit when it does reach people?#

ILDA describes the principles in general terms:189

  • A wider beam. A lens that increases divergence puts the same power over more area.
  • Less power near people. Filters, beam splitters or a software attenuation map dim the part of the scan field that reaches the crowd.
  • A cutoff if the scan fails. ILDA's target for the safeguard is shut-off within 10 ms, preferably 1 ms.
  • Measurement. Irradiance is checked at the nearest audience position with every color at full show level.
  • A trained person watching. An operator with an emergency stop watches the whole time.

Has anyone been hurt at a laser show?#

Yes, and the best-known case is still argued over. At the Aquamarine Open Air Festival in Kirzhach, Russia, in July 2008, attendees said heavy rain pushed organizers to put up large tents, and beams meant for the sky reached the crowd.201 The newspaper Kommersant, relayed by Reuters, quoted a treating ophthalmologist: "They all have retinal burns, scarring is visible on them."201

Almost every other detail comes in two versions. Reuters dated the event July 5 and put Kirzhach 50 miles northeast of Moscow; Photonics.com, citing Kommersant, gave July 6 and about 100 miles.201202 Reuters counted 12 patients confirmed by Moscow health officials at one clinic plus 17 more that Kommersant reported at a city hospital, 29 in all; Photonics.com reported more than 30, aged 16 to 30.201202 By July 16, Photonics.com reported, Kommersant had revised its count to 12, all with hemorrhages around the retina.203 A 2020 editorial in a retina trade journal put it at about 30 people seeking care and 12 cases of laser retinopathy reported by the Moscow Health Department.204

Russian officials said a laser aimed into the audience several times, against safety rules, was the possible cause. Some Moscow ophthalmologists questioned the laser explanation, with an alternative theory the follow-up report called unproven.203 A regional government representative told the Associated Press the festival lacked proper permits, while an organizer said the hired laser company held safety certificates.202 The 2020 editorial says high-powered pulsed lasers were used; that detail rests on the editorial alone.204 None of the coverage cites an official technical finding on the lasers.

The medical journals hold smaller cases. In South Korea in 2014, two 20-year-old women lost vision to 20/800 after a laser show at the same club on consecutive days; both had surgery and recovered 20/20. The device was reported as a 510 nm green diode laser labeled 2 mW, Class 3R, the operators denied modifying it, and the authors suggested repeated exposure over hours as a factor.205 A 2016 Turkish paper described three macular injuries from non-medical lasers - at a wedding, at a hotel (a 9-year-old) and at an entertainment venue. Two recovered near-normal vision after surgery, one was left with permanent loss, and the laser types and powers were never established.187

For scale, the Rockwell database counted 241 eye incidents across all laser uses from 1964 to 1996, of which 16 involved spectators and 11 involved laser show operators. O'Hagan noted that no dedicated incident data existed for entertainment lasers.196

Can a laser show damage a phone or camera?#

Yes. The eye limit does not protect cameras: no MPE has been written for camera sensors, a camera lens can gather more light than an eye and focus it to a finer point, and ILDA's summary is that sensors "are, in general, more susceptible to damage than the human eye."206 Damage runs from a few dead pixels to dead rows and columns to burned-in patterns.206

ILDA reports thresholds from a March 2017 paper in Optical Engineering: roughly 40,000 to 60,000 W/cm2 for color CMOS sensors, about 16,000 W/cm2 for CCDs at short exposures, and about 19,000 W/cm2 for the mirror chips in video projectors.206 Its case list includes Canon 5D Mark II and HP Photosmart 945 sensors, and 12 video projectors worth over $100,000 damaged when a truss was not raised to height.206 Camera damage was reported at Aquamarine too.203

A concertgoer's phone camera was left with permanent black lines after a beam entered the lens at a show in Naples, Italy, on April 19, 2023, PetaPixel reported; the same piece quotes Sony's 2021 notice for its cameras: "Do not directly expose the Lens to beams such as laser beams."207 A leaked Apple repair guide from September 2014 listed laser contact with the camera as damage outside warranty.208

The highest risk is a beam straight into the lens. ILDA says shots of beams in mid-air or of graphics on a screen should not cause damage, that producers "cannot be responsible for audience-member damage to cameras", and that for fixed cameras the laser operator should keep beams off the lens.206

Who is responsible on site?#

In the US, the person holding the variance. The FDA's position since 1977 is that assembling a show from an existing laser makes a new product, so the show producer is legally a manufacturer.28 The variance conditions then require an operator who directly controls the show and can stop it at once if conditions become unsafe.197

The Laser Safety Officer (LSO) is the named person responsible for laser safety. State rules in New York, Texas, Massachusetts and Illinois require or name one, and in Massachusetts the officer is responsible for shutting the laser down if conditions turn unsafe.209210211212 Germany requires a Laserschutzbeauftragter, appointed in writing, before Class 3R, 3B or 4 lasers are operated, unless the employer has the required expertise personally.213 ILDA's 2025 survey of requirements says the US federal variance form mentions an LSO only where audience scanning is approved, and that the UK has no formal qualification.18

Standards and trade practice fill in the rest. ANSI Z136.1 is the general US standard for safe laser use, and Z136.10 covers entertainment, display and exhibition settings.214 ILDA's draft Category A standard defines four roles - Employer, Laser Safety Officer, Laser Operator and Laser Spotter - and its one-page US overview describes a trained operator who can see every beam path or uses a spotter, with emergency stops within reach.195178 Outdoors, the FAA's advisory circular describes a safety observer who can terminate the beam immediately.215

Are laser shows safe to watch?#

The regulators' answer is that a compliant show is designed so the audience never meets a hazardous beam. The FDA says shows "keep hazardous lasers away from the audience", and that where a beam could fall on the crowd, power is kept below hazardous levels.28 HSE says display lasers need controls against significant eye injury, and lists scan-failure safety systems, emergency stops, barriers and competent operators.30

The record backs that up without making it a guarantee. Sixteen spectator eye incidents in a 32-year database and ILDA's "essentially no injuries" estimate sit beside Kirzhach, a Seoul club and three Turkish cases.196189201205187 No dedicated count of show injuries existed when O'Hagan went looking in 1998, so nobody can put a rate on it.196

Cue 08

The rules: who regulates laser shows, country by country

The short version: In the United States a show laser above 5 mW of visible output is lawful only under a variance from the FDA, and a beam that leaves for open sky brings in the FAA. Most other countries have no laser show license at all: general workplace safety law and the same exposure limit apply, and guidance documents carry the practical detail. Past the US, UK, Germany, Canada and Australia, the country-by-country picture comes from a list the trade association itself calls unofficial.

Who regulates laser shows in the United States?#

The Food and Drug Administration, through its Center for Devices and Radiological Health (CDRH). The authority is the Federal Food, Drug and Cosmetic Act, Chapter V (electronic product radiation control), with records and reporting rules in 21 CFR Parts 1000 to 1005 and the performance standards in Parts 1010, 1040.10 and 1040.11.28

Three pieces of that are law and do most of the work:

  • 21 CFR 1040.10(b)(13) defines a demonstration laser product as one made, designed, intended or promoted for demonstration, entertainment, advertising display or artistic composition.6
  • 21 CFR 1040.11(c) says such products must meet the requirements for Class I, IIa, II or IIIa, and "shall not permit human access to laser radiation in excess of the accessible emission limits of Class I" and, where applicable, IIa, II or IIIa. The paragraph's source note cites 50 FR 33702, August 20, 1985.216
  • The effect, in the FDA's own summary: projectors are limited to 5 mW of visible output unless the FDA approves a variance, and Class IIIb and IV light show equipment needs one.28

One piece of guidance set the shape of the industry. Laser Notice 22, dated November 22 or 23, 1977 depending on which FDA page is read, said light shows fall under the standard and that assembling a show from an existing laser makes a "new" product - so the show producer is a manufacturer.28217

New York got there first. Its Industrial Code Rule 50 on lasers took effect on August 1, 1972.209

What is an FDA laser variance, and how is one applied for?#

A variance is, in the FDA's words, "permission to vary from one or more requirements of a performance standard", requested under 21 CFR 1010.4. For shows the agency calls it "an alternate means of providing radiation safety".21828

There are three forms: the Product Report (Form FDA 3632), which describes the projector; the Laser Light Show Report (Form FDA 3640), which describes the show; and the Variance Application (Form FDA 3147).28 A show producer files the application and the show report. A projector manufacturer files all three. A buyer of an already-certified projector needs only the show report and the application.28 Since September 1, 2020 the package goes to CDRH by email, with no separate copy to Dockets Management.218 Production or shows may begin only after the approval letter arrives, and the letter sets the conditions.28

The form in its 04/24 revision says the FDA generally approves for two years.197 Annual reports under 21 CFR 1002.13 are due September 1 and cover July 1 to June 30.198 Under Laser Notice 55 (2007, guidance), eligible Class IIIb and IV show variances roll over a year at a time, to December 31, as long as that year's report is filed; miss it and the variance ends December 31. Audience scanning, invisible-laser effects and reduced clearance distances are excluded from that streamlined path.198

Selling is covered too. Laser Notice 51 (2001, guidance) says manufacturers may deliver projectors only to customers holding an approved variance, that dealers and distributors need their own, and that a dealer's demonstration counts as producing a light show.219 At the border, Import Alert 95-04, first issued in December 1997 and revised July 31, 2026, lets staff detain without physical examination laser pointers, light show projectors and similar products from listed firms. Other covered products can be detained when an examination shows, among other things, output above 5 mW, a missing warning label or no product report.220

International conformance does not open a side door. Laser Notice 56, issued May 8, 2019 and reissued February 21, 2023 (the FDA's index page dates it January 19, 2018), says the agency does not intend to enforce parts of 1040.10 and 1040.11 for products that conform to the comparable clauses of IEC 60825-1 Edition 3. Its section III.B lists 1040.11(c) and 1010.4 as not covered, so the Class IIIa demonstration limit and the variance requirement stand.221217

The system is old. In 2006 ILDA's then executive director wrote in Laser Focus World that the display rules "date back almost 30 years" and had "remained virtually unchanged".222

How high do laser beams have to be above the audience?#

In the US, 3.0 meters. That figure is a condition of the variance, not a line in the regulation: it is printed on Form FDA 3147 among the protections an applicant commits to, and it binds the holder. Item 13d says laser radiation above Class I limits is not permitted at any point less than 3.0 meters above any surface where people other than operators, performers or employees may stand, or within 2.5 meters below or laterally from where such persons may be.197

Other standard conditions on the same form:197

  • Item 13c: no scanning, projection or reflection into audiences or accessible uncontrolled areas "except for diffuse reflections produced by the atmosphere", added scattering media and target screens.
  • Trained operators must directly control the show, be placed where every beam path can be seen, and stop it at once if anything is unsafe; output must not exceed what the effects need.
  • Written setup, alignment and test procedures, and records under 21 CFR 1002.30 and 1002.31.
  • Marked and controlled laser areas, to ANSI Z136.1 or equivalent.
  • Scanning safeguards wherever scanning is relied on to meet the limits.
  • Projectors may be passed only to someone who holds a variance.
  • Operators, performers and employees may not be required or allowed to view radiation above Class I limits.
  • Item 13l: notice to the FAA for projections into open airspace "at any time", setup, alignment and rehearsal included.

What does the FAA have to do with a laser show?#

The FAA regulates navigable airspace under 49 U.S.C. 40103, and it does not permit or license shows. It reviews a notice and issues a letter of determination that says either "FAA has no objection" or that it objects.215 The force comes from the FDA side, where FAA notification is a variance condition.197 The FAA's own document, Advisory Circular 70-1B, "Outdoor Laser Operations" (February 14, 2022, with a change dated May 5, 2022), is guidance.215

The circular applies to anyone planning outdoor visible beams (400 to 700 nm) above 50 nW/cm2 in navigable airspace, or any beam above the MPE there. The filing is FAA Form 7140-1, "Notice of Proposed Outdoor Laser Operation(s)", with a Laser Configuration Worksheet for each configuration, sent to the service center for the area "at least 30 days before the planned event".215 ILDA's one-page overview tells members to submit at least 90 days ahead and says review may take 30 to 90 days; that is trade advice, and the FAA's figure is 30.178

Airspace near airports is divided into zones with brightness caps:

Zone Limit (AC 70-1B, 2022) Extent (FAA Order JO 7400.2R)
Laser Free Zone 50 nW/cm2 or less Within 2 NM of the runway centerline up to 2,000 ft above ground, plus a 3 NM extension 2,500 ft each side of the extended centerline
Critical Flight Zone 5 microwatts/cm2 or less - a level that should not cause veiling glare 10 NM radius from the airport reference point, up to 10,000 ft above ground
Sensitive Flight Zone 100 microwatts/cm2 or less Locally designated; no fixed size
Normal Flight Zone The MPE Everything else

The limits are from the circular's Appendix A and Appendix F; the dimensions are from paragraph 29-1-5 of the air traffic order.215190

The units are easy to misread. The Critical and Sensitive limits are in microwatts per square centimeter. The Advisory Circular spells the unit out - "100 microwatts per square centimeter" for the Sensitive zone - and its glossary gives 5 and 100 with the microwatt symbol. The FAA's web text of Order JO 7400.2R, paragraph 29-1-5, prints the same two limits as "5mW/cm2" and "100mW/cm2", a thousand times larger, which would put both caps above the 2.6 mW/cm2 exposure limit given in the same list. The table uses the circular's microwatts.215190

The circular also describes safety observers - people who can terminate the beam immediately - and allows an electronic protection system in their place if the proponent documents that it meets the performance criteria in SAE AS6029. It says the FAA's interest "does not supersede or invalidate any existing rules or ordinances" of other governments.215

Do US states have their own laser show rules?#

Four have regulations on the books that mention shows or mobile lasers. All four are law.

State Rule What it requires
New York 12 NYCRR Part 50 (Industrial Code Rule 50), Department of Labor; in effect since 1972, amended 1994 Registration of laser installations and mobile lasers (50.8); a certificate of competence to operate a mobile laser (50.9), Class A for low-intensity and Class B for high- and low-intensity, with applicants at least 18, a year's experience and an exam; certificates last three years; owners name a laser safety officer (50.11). No section specific to light shows.209
Texas 25 Texas Administrative Code 289.301, Department of State Health Services; latest amendment effective December 17, 2024 Registration of Class 3B and 4 lasers; a registration certificate before any light show, plus a valid FDA variance or evidence of the FDA annual report; written notice on RC Form 301-05 seven days before each show, signed by the laser safety officer.210
Illinois 32 Ill. Adm. Code 315.140, Illinois Emergency Management Agency; amended effective December 9, 2013 Written notice at least 10 working days before a show, with FDA variance documents and, outdoors, a copy of the FAA notification. Audience locations limited to Class 1, scan failure included; nothing above Class 2 less than 3 meters above audience standing surfaces or within 2.5 meters laterally; direct operator supervision unless output is under 5 mW or beams are at least 6 meters up or 2.5 meters laterally separated.212
Massachusetts 105 CMR 121, Department of Public Health Radiation Control Program Registration of facilities with Class 3B or 4 lasers and a named laser safety officer; written notice at least 10 working days before temporary use of an out-of-state laser (121.022). For entertainment lasers (121.008): audience areas limited to Class 1; separations of 10 feet above and 8 feet to the side or below under continuous operator control, 20 feet above without it; an FDA variance; FAA notification copied to the program.211

Beyond those, ILDA's overview lists Arizona and Florida for notification or registration, and registration with and inspection by the fire department in Cleveland, Orlando, Palm Springs and Portland. That is the trade body's summary, not the agencies' own text.178

Is it illegal to point a laser at an aircraft?#

In the US it is a federal crime. Under 18 U.S.C. 39A, added by Public Law 112-95 on February 14, 2012, knowingly aiming a laser pointer beam at an aircraft, or at its flight path, is punishable by a fine, up to five years in prison, or both. The exceptions are FAA-authorized research and flight test, Defense and Homeland Security activity, and emergency laser signaling devices.223 The FAA gives the figures as criminal penalties of up to five years and $250,000, and civil fines of up to $11,000 per violation.224

Year Laser incidents reported to the FAA (official)
2016 7,398
2017 6,754
2018 5,663
2019 6,136
2020 6,852
2021 9,723
2022 9,457
2023 13,304
2024 12,840
2025 10,993

The FAA's table has 10,993 for 2025; its February 2026 press release says 10,994 and calls it a 14% fall from 2024.225224 The release counts 337 pilot injuries reported since 2010, and puts California first among states in 2025 with 1,309 reports, then Texas with 1,100.224 The FAA does not break out shows in these numbers; the statute is written around laser pointers.223225

Canada counted 1,965 reported laser attacks on aircraft from January 2015 to December 2019, and in the UK endangering an aircraft is an offense under article 240 of the Air Navigation Order 2016.226227

Who regulates laser shows in the UK?#

Nobody issues a laser show license. HSE's position is that duty holders must assess and control the risks and use competent contractors, under general health and safety law; local authorities enforce that law in entertainment premises and handle entertainment licensing.30196

The dedicated guidance has been withdrawn. HS(G)95, "The radiation safety of lasers used for display purposes", was published by HSE in October 1996 to replace a 1980 guidance note, PM19.191 HSE now says HS(G)95 "has been withdrawn" and points to the trade association PLASA's "Guidance for the Safety of Display Lasers".30 PLASA describes that guide as prepared with trade bodies, laser display specialists, venues, local authority environmental health officers, insurers, HSE and Public Health England. It is not compulsory, PLASA says, but following it will normally be enough to comply with the law, and inspectors may cite it as good practice.228 That makes it industry guidance with the regulator's backing.

Consumer sales are a separate matter: the UK Health Security Agency says consumer laser products fall under BS EN 50689:2021, and reports the standard as saying Class 3B and Class 4 lasers "are not suitable for general use by consumers".29

For outdoor beams, the Civil Aviation Authority has two documents that disagree on timing. CAP 736, version 3 of February 1, 2011 and still listed as current, says organizers should give 28 days' advance notification on form DAP 1918 - the sample form in its annex says that notice "MUST be given" - and says the UK "does not prescribe precise dimensions" for beam zones.229 The CAA's current web page says "A minimum of 7 days' notice is requested", that it normally issues a NOTAM and does not charge, and that the legal backstop is articles 240, 241 and 265 of the Air Navigation Order 2016.227 The web page cites the 2016 Order, so it is the newer of the two.

How does Germany regulate laser shows?#

Through occupational safety law, with an officer at the center. Section 5(2) of the OStrV, the workplace ordinance on artificial optical radiation, requires an employer who lacks the required expertise personally to appoint a Laserschutzbeauftragter in writing before operating Class 3R, 3B or 4 lasers. The officer's qualification is shown by passing a course and kept current through further training. The officer supports the risk assessment and the protective measures and monitors safe operation.213

The show-specific detail is in guidance from the statutory accident insurers: DGUV Information 203-036, on laser equipment for show and projection use, January 2021. It defines the audience area as wherever the legal exposure limit values are not exceeded for the whole show, and asks that the limits hold up to 2.70 meters where people can be. The show area is separated by a stage, pit or barrier at least 0.8 meters high with at least 1 meter of lateral distance, and a competent person inspects before first use and after each rebuild and verifies the limits on site for every planned effect.199 It also warns that lasers classified under the 2015 edition of EN 60825-1 can exceed the exposure limits even in Class 1.199

What are the rules in Canada?#

Health Canada's guidance covers laser products, not shows as such. The Canada Consumer Product Safety Act is used against Class 3B and 4 handheld portable lasers; the national standard CAN/CSA E60825-1:15 is identical to IEC 60825-1 (2014) apart from added electrical code requirements; and meeting the US rules in 21 CFR 1040.10 and 1040.11 does not automatically meet Canadian requirements. Installation, operation and training rules sit with the provinces and territories.193

The sky is federal law. Aiming a laser into navigable airspace needs written permission from Transport Canada, sought through a "Notice of proposal to conduct outdoor laser operation(s)"; the prohibition on projecting a bright light source into navigable airspace without authorization is in Canadian Aviation Regulations 601.20.230226 Since 2020, possessing a hand-held laser over 1 mW without a legitimate reason is prohibited outside a private dwelling in the greater Montreal, Toronto and Vancouver regions and within 10 km of any airport or certified heliport, with fines of $1,000 on the spot and up to $5,000 for an individual or $25,000 for a corporation.230226

What about Australia, Japan and everywhere else?#

Australia's aviation regulator, CASA, says organizers "must ensure that the lasers and lights don't pose an aviation safety risk" and points them to its Advisory Circular AC 139-23 and to state and local approvals.231 In South Australia, SafeWork SA issued a safety alert on April 5, 2024 citing growing concern about unsafe use of Class 3B and 4 lasers at entertainment venues. It cites regulation 223(5) of the Work Health and Safety Regulations 2012 on operator training, and says lasers must not be directed where the beam could reach a person, a reflective surface or anything ignitable.232

Japan's JIS C 6802 is the counterpart of IEC 60825-1, and its consumer product safety law covers portable lasers such as pointers; neither is written for professional show systems.192233

For most of the rest of the world, the only compiled reference is ILDA's "Worldwide audience scanning laws" page, which says of itself that it is "NOT official and is not regularly updated". Its main table dates from mid-2014, with some entries revised since.200 What follows is ILDA's account, not the regulators'. Switzerland: since December 2020 only holders of a Swiss certificate may run show lasers, with a higher qualification for audience scanning, and events are reported to the federal health office 14 days ahead. Sweden: a permit from the radiation authority, with scanning declared alongside a risk assessment and on-site measurement. Finland: a permit, and rules that prohibit audience scanning apart from some special permits. Austria: a permit. Netherlands: no show-laser law beyond one on pointers, with practitioners informally keeping the lowest beams at least 3 meters up. France: some regions reported to prohibit Class 4 show lasers. Japan: listed among countries where a permit is required "but this law is often not followed or enforced".200

What are the laser show rules in each country?#

See also the rules by place table.

Australia CASA; state regulators AC 139-23; South Australia WHS Regulations 2012231232 Guidance, law -
Japan METI (consumer lasers) JIS C 6802192233 Standard, law for portable devices Permit required but often not followed or enforced, per ILDA's unofficial list200
Switzerland, Sweden, Finland, Austria, Netherlands, France - Entries on ILDA's unofficial list only200 Unverified As described above

What is law, what is guidance, and what is industry practice?#

Law is the short list. In the US: the class limits and hardware rules of 21 CFR 1040.10, the 5 mW demonstration cap of 1040.11(c), the state regulations, and 18 U.S.C. 39A.6216223 In Germany, the OStrV and its laser safety officer.213 In Canada, the aviation regulations.230 In the UK, general duties and the Air Navigation Order, with nothing written for shows.30227

Conditions sit one step down and bind only the holder: the 3.0 and 2.5 meter separations, the default bar on audience scanning and the duty to notify the FAA all come from the FDA's variance form.197

Guidance is what regulators publish without the force of a rule - the FDA's Laser Notices, the FAA's Advisory Circular, the CAA's CAP 736, DGUV Information 203-036.217215229199

Standards are consensus documents, voluntary unless a law adopts them: IEC 60825-1 and its national copies, the technical report IEC TR 60825-3 for displays and shows, ANSI Z136.1 and Z136.10.29228214

Industry practice is the trade's own: ILDA's audience scanning ceilings, its draft Category A standard - 3.0 meters above and 2.5 meters lateral, a 6 W draft maximum per beam, marked "NOT YET AN OFFICIAL ILDA POLICY" - and PLASA's guide.189195228

Cue 09

On stage: tours, festivals, companies and costs

The short version: Pink Floyd's 1994 US tour carried two 50 W laser systems; by 2025 the Guinness record stood at 1,100 separate laser sources in one stadium. The same short list of specialist companies turns up in the credits, and they publish gear lists far more readily than prices. Ticket prices, a US paperwork fee of zero and a few manufacturer list prices are public. Day rates, insurance and the size of the whole business are not.

Which tours carried lasers, and what were the rigs?#

Gear lists come from trade magazines, which print what suppliers tell them. For how a rig like this sits inside the rest of a production, see how a dance music show is built.

  • Pink Floyd, 1994 US tour. Lighting designer Marc Brickman had "2 complete, 50W, copper-vapor laser systems", built by Oxford Lasers and supplied by Rocklite of Toronto, with Warren "Wiggy" Toll operating and Allen Domanski programming. The Lighting Dimensions article that lists them survives online as a fan-archive transcript.234
  • Roger Waters, Dark Side of the Moon tour, 2007. Partway through the tour, for the Mexico dates, Brickman added a rotating 3D prism drawn in the air by five direct-diode solid-state lasers totaling over 100 W, flown from cranes on a rig of roughly 2,500 lb. Lightwave International and Excitement Technologies Group built it; Martin Potoczny programmed and ran it on Pangolin software with grandMA playback. ILDA's Patrick Murphy told the trade press at the time: "Solid-state lasers are revolutionizing laser effects."235
  • Rush, Snakes & Arrows tour, 2007. Two Laser Scope 60 W YAG lasers and two custom projectors from Production Design International, with Andrew Seabeck as laser tech. This list also survives as a fan-archive transcript, of a PLSN profile.236
  • Tool, 2024 US and Canada tour. Lasers by Production Design International, which has worked with the band since 2009; Scott Wilson is credited as laser designer, programmer and operator.237183
  • Jean-Michel Jarre, 2017. 13 Laser Image Supernova HD 35 W OPSL lasers, 16 RGB grating boxes and a 20 W laser harp - a fan of beams wired as a MIDI controller, so that breaking a beam with a hand triggers a synthesizer note. Hugo Bunk of Laser Image supplied the lasers, run on Pangolin Beyond and FB4.238 Jarre had been working at scale for decades: his Rendez-vous Houston concert on April 5, 1986 drew 1.3 million people by PLSN's count and 1.5 million by Wikipedia's, and Guinness listed it as the largest outdoor concert.23872
  • Coldplay, Music of the Spheres. X-Laser Triton 10 (10 W) and Triton 36 (36 W) units supplied through Strictly FX. Nobody has printed how many.239
  • The 2023 arena class. PLSN's laser roundup that year listed The Weeknd's After Hours Til Dawn tour with several Kvant 25 W lasers from Pyrotecnico FX, programmed by Tyler Barbone; Drake with 10 ISFX 100 W lasers and six Kvant 30 W units from ImageSFX, four of them mounted on robots; and Aerosmith with 44 lasers of 30 W each planned. KISS's End of the Road tour, by Lightwave International's own figures, carried more than 200 lasers and 2,000 W.183

Planetarium laser shows, their music licensing and the 2026 Phoebe Bridgers "Lost Weekend" run in 44 planetariums are a separate trade with separate economics, covered in the planetarium chapter.104

How many lasers does a festival or a halftime show use?#

More each decade, and the numbers nearly always come from the people who supplied the lasers or the software.

The 2019 EDC rig ran with Pangolin's PASS safety hardware, and the vendor calls it "what is considered to be the world's largest outdoor audience scanning event" - the vendor's description, not a certified record.242 Audience scanning, where beams are deliberately swept through the crowd at controlled exposure levels, is its own subject in the safety chapter.

Sphere is known for its LED screen, but touring acts do bring show lasers into it. The software maker puts Illenium's total at "dozens" without giving a number.246 At the Paris 2024 opening ceremony on July 26, press coverage described projection mapping and a laser show on the Eiffel Tower, and nobody has published the count, the wattage or the supplier.247248

What is the biggest laser show ever?#

It depends on what gets counted, and the counting unit has changed three times in eight years. The same shows sit alongside others in the landmark shows table.

Date Who Where Count Unit counted
Nov 18, 2017 ER Productions Outside the Las Vegas Convention Center, during the LDI trade show 314, at 1,377 W in the closing sequence laser fixtures249250
Oct 17, 2019 Image Engineering Staples Center, Los Angeles 642 laser beams250
2021 Dubai Racing Club Closing ceremony of the Dubai World Cup 824 as reported by Mixmag251
July 19, 2025 Verknipt ArenA Johan Cruijff ArenA, Amsterdam 1,100 laser emission sources252

The 2017 show was a seven-minute sequence, and it took about 13 tons of equipment and 6.8 miles of cable, with kit a Kent newspaper valued at £2.6 million.253254 The 2019 record was certified on site by Guinness judge Andrew Glass.250

The current record is Guinness's "Largest laser show": 1,100 laser emission sources in front of about 40,000 people. Guinness counts each point where a beam leaves a unit, and a beam split by a diffraction grating counts once.252 The record piece ran 10 minutes.251 The rig mixed RGB units above 30 W with single-color units and beam lasers, and the published safety measures read like a checklist: a risk analysis per device, a laser safety officer, compliance with DIN EN 60825-1, emergency stops, automatic beam interruption and sign-off by outside testers. The laser supplier has not been named.255

Guinness keeps a second category for ground covered. That one belongs to "Pulse of Light" by Martin Arnaud at Noor Riyadh on November 15, 2022: 14,459,160 square meters.256

Several of the specialists also sell pyrotechnics, flames and cryo jets, and several build their own projectors. Award tallies and show counts in the table are the companies' own.

How much does a laser show cost?#

The hardware has list prices if you know where to look. The service does not.

What Figure Year Status
FDA variance (US permission to run a Class IIIb or IV show) no government fee 2017 official, per an ILDA-affiliated guide262
X-Laser "EZ Variance Kit", an online application for that maker's own US products $59 (list $79) 2026 retail price263
Kvant Clubmax 10 FB4, a 10 W projector 8,582 EUR before VAT 2026 manufacturer page metadata; the visible page says "price on request"16
Kvant Beam Brush 40, 37 W guaranteed 30,700 EUR before VAT, options 150 to 1,429 EUR 2026 manufacturer page metadata; "price on request" on the page264
Krypton gas system for a laser dome $100,000 1970s reported41
Laser Images (Laserium) retail projector models $60,000 to $250,000 2006 reported80
Kit used in the 2017 record £2.6 million 2017 reported254
ILDA accreditation $395 to apply for members, $595 for others; then $1,000 or $1,800 for four years 2026 official265
Planetarium laser show ticket $12 to $18 2009 to 2026 official and reported98592102107

The FDA's own laser light show page lists the variance forms and no fee, and the industry guide warns that a new application "can require several months" of review.28262 What the variance is and who needs one is in the rules chapter.

The only public worked example of how a laser company arrives at a day rate is an old one. L. Michael Roberts's LaserFX business pages, written between about 1996 and 2008, take a $50,000 system written off over 2.5 years: it has to earn about $20,000 a year, or roughly $1,000 per show-day at 20 shows a year. Add overhead and a 25% margin and his example lands near $2,750 per show-day, with later days at the same site billed at half.266 It shows the arithmetic in dollars of that era; it is not a quote.

No vendor publishes a current day rate for a club laser with an operator, a festival laser package, a planetarium show lease or show-control software; they quote privately. Insurance premiums for laser shows are not published anywhere citable. Nobody has produced a credible estimate of the size of the show laser business. The number that circulates - a "laser projector" market of USD 15.5 billion in 2023, forecast to reach USD 69.9 billion by 2032 - is one research firm's press-release estimate, and it describes video projectors with laser light sources for cinemas, homes and classrooms.267 It says nothing about beams in haze.

What is ILDA, and what are the ILDA Awards?#

The International Laser Display Association is the trade body, founded at an organizational meeting in Lake Tahoe on August 16, 1986, with Ron Goldstein as president, Walt Meador as secretary and Tim Walsh as treasurer.74 It has four membership categories - corporate, nonprofit, individual and student - and members get awards eligibility, conference access, free use of the Skyzan laser safety software, the member logo and recourse to an ethics committee.268

The ILDA Awards are the industry's annual prizes; how they are judged is in the programming chapter. The artistic categories include Beams/Atmospherics, Graphics, Abstract, Planetarium, Corporate, Live Stage, Live TV, Laser Photography and Laser Jockey. The Fenning Award goes to technical achievement, and the Career Achievement Award is the highest honor. In 2014 there were 204 entries from 38 member companies, a record number of entrants, and the ceremony was held in Las Vegas on November 19.261 The first Career Achievement Award went to Laserium's founder Ivan Dryer; ILDA's own page gives the year as both 1988 and 1989.269

ILDA also runs an accreditation, the ILDA Accredited Professional Lasershow Company. A company needs at least 15 documented shows over two years, an award or two award entries, three client references, an employee who has passed a Laser Safety Officer course and a signed safety affirmation. ILDA membership is not required. Five companies hold it: Lightwave International, LOBO electronic, Laser Production Network, Laser Spectacles and Dynamic Lasers.265 It is an industry credential, separate from any government permission.

Is laser art in galleries a different thing from a laser show?#

Same light, different job.

Rockne Krebs (1938-2011) moved from Plexiglas sculpture to laser light in 1967 with "Sculpture Minus Object" at the Washington Gallery of Modern Art: red beams, fog and mirrors, and nothing else in the room. He worked with Hewlett-Packard physicists in 1969, showed in the US pavilion at Expo '70 in Osaka, and went on to make more than 30 large public light works.270 In September 1969 he filed US patent 3,622,228, which ILDA calls apparently the first patent for laser artistic display. His 1983 piece "The Green Hypotenuse" ran a single beam 7 miles from Mount Wilson to Caltech.38

Hiro Yamagata put "Photon 999" on the Guggenheim Museum Bilbao from October 31 to December 2, 2001: 15 lasers projecting onto a structure on the facade, with a walkway through the light.271 The beams, from tarm Showlaser, Times of Change and Dymax, were aimed at more than 200 mirrors on 20 m truss towers.272

Yvette Mattern's "Global Rainbow" is seven parallel beams in the colors of the spectrum, first shown in New York in 2009 and since taken to Berlin, three UK sites for the London 2012 Cultural Olympiad, Toronto's Nuit Blanche in 2014 and Pittsburgh, where Lightwave International supplied the lasers.273258

Chris Levine's "Higher Power" sent one green 515 nm beam from San Clemente island across Venice from May 4 to 11, 2026, during the Biennale's opening week.274 Rita McBride's "Portal", at the 2016 Liverpool Biennial, crossed beams inside the Toxteth Reservoir; the projector maker Kvant says the piece used 16 green modules of 1 W each at 520 nm.275

Cue 10

Lasers at festivals and big events

The short version: A festival laser rig is mostly a list of Kvant model numbers and a software license, and most of what is known about any rig comes from the company that sold the projectors or the software. The best documented nights are Coachella 2025, Glastonbury 2019, Illenium's 147-laser stadium show in 2024 and a run of Super Bowl halftimes. Outdoors in the US, every one of them also needed a letter from the FAA, and whether the beams may touch the crowd depends on which side of the Atlantic the field is on.

The headline counts for EDC, the halftimes and the Guinness records, and the companies behind them, are in /stage/. What follows is the detail.

Who does the lasers for Coachella?#

In 2025, Polar Productions, on six stages and at Stagecoach. It lit Quasar, Sahara, Yuma, the main stage, Gobi and Mojave, and put lasers into Stagecoach for the first time, at Diplo's Honky Tonk.244

The model list reads like a Kvant catalog: Atom 42, BeamBrush 35, BeamBrush 10 with PASS, and Clubmax 24, 10 and 6800 with PASS, all run from Pangolin's BEYOND software and FB4 network hardware.244 PASS, the Professional Audience Safety System, is what let selected units on the main stage, Gobi and Mojave point into the crowd. Quasar used six Kvant Atom units in a circle as an aerial marker.244 The case study is Pangolin's own account, and it gives no wattages, no total count and no designers.

Three years earlier the headline laser rig came with the act. Swedish House Mafia's Coachella 2022 lasers were supplied by ER Productions and programmed by Derek Abbott in BEYOND, with the Depence visualizer and FB4. The projectors sat in a circular structure over the DJs to throw aerial beams, and the design went on tour afterward. That account is also Pangolin's.276

How many lasers were at EDC and Ultra?#

At EDC Las Vegas in 2014, the Kinetic Field main stage ran more than 30 high-power laser systems. Lasertainment, Laserworld's show partner, supplied RTI NANO and RTI PIKO projectors, controlled by a Lasergraph DSP with a grandMA2 lighting console on top. No wattage, no designer.241

Miami's Ultra Music Festival published a similar number a year later. In 2015 LaserNet (Laser Production Network), a Miami company, ran "over 30 laser projectors" across seven stages at Bayfront Park, every one above 20 W and every one on BEYOND.277 The source is Pangolin's own case study, and it names no models.

What did Glastonbury's headliners bring in 2019?#

Glastonbury publishes no laser plan, but ER Productions published what it hung for four acts that year. These are the company's own fixture lists, without wattages or designers.278

Act Stage Fixtures
Stormzy Pyramid Stage, Friday headliner 12 BB4, 8 AT-30, 1 Kinekt
Tame Impala Other Stage 14 AT-30, 8 AT-20, 10 BB4, 8 Phaenon Pro 30000
The Chemical Brothers Saturday headliner 18 Kinekt, 12 Viper deLuxe, 4 Beamburst, 1 Phaenon X Pro 30000, 1 Tripan
Hot Chip Park Stage 20 Beamburst, 6 EX15, 6 Viper deLuxe

The Chemical Brothers' 18 Kinekts are the most of any single model on the list, and the Pyramid headliner's rig was the smallest of the four.278

What lasers did Illenium use at SoFi Stadium?#

On February 2 and 3, 2024, Illenium played his Trilogy shows at SoFi Stadium in Los Angeles.279 Pangolin's case study puts the rig at 147 laser systems totaling more than 4,000 W. Most were Kvant Atom 42s, with BeamBrush 35, Clubmax 6800 and 6000 units and a featured Kvant EPIC rated at 270 W. Brad Kellogg was lead designer and Bill Loftus laser programmer, working with Polar Productions.280

The same page calls it "the highest wattage laser show ever witnessed".280 That is the vendor's line, not a certified record. Nobody certifies watts; the Guinness record a year later counted emission sources instead.

Swedish House Mafia's "3.0 era" opened in Mexico City in 2025 at the Plaza de Toros with 12 Kvant Atom 58, 12 Atom 42 and two Spectrum 55 units, 26 in all. InterlaserMX designed, programmed and operated it for the promoter OCESA, by Pangolin's account.281 A smaller bass festival shows the same supply chain at another scale: Pangolin says Forbidden Kingdom 2026 ran Kvant Spectrum 25 and 30 and Clubmax 40 units through Hypnotic Productions, a South Florida firm, with no counts.282

Do lasers at Sphere hit the screen?#

Some are aimed at it, and some are aimed to stop. For Illenium's "Odyssey" at Sphere in Las Vegas, a show the trade write-up does not date, 16 Kvant Atom 58 W projectors were "positioned to interact with the venue's immersive screen", and four more on the lower mezzanine handled beam termination - ending beams on a surface so they go no farther. The lasers were zoned so the beams looked symmetrical from every seat. Bill Loftus did system design, programming and operation.245

Anyma's "The End of Genesys" ran at Sphere from February 27 to March 2, 2025, with "sharp, precise laser beams" moving with the drops, in Digital Music News's description. No supplier is named.283

What does a Super Bowl halftime laser rig look like?#

Wet, mostly. For The Who at Super Bowl XLIV, Sun Life Stadium in Miami, February 7, 2010, Laser Design Productions ran 16 of its LDP 10 projectors: four at 50 W, four at 25 W, four at 20 W and four at 13 W, which adds to about 432 W. The trade report rounds it to more than 400 W. Doug Adams, the company's president, designed it, Victor Tomei was laser technical director and Jason McEachern programmed. Rain housings, vibration-isolating platforms and generators kept the gear alive on a football field.284 Pangolin's account of the same night, in the stage table, splits the 16 another way: eight full-color units on radial carts, four 50 W YAGs and four air-cooled full-color units.240

For Usher at Super Bowl LVIII in 2024, Pangolin's case study adds the lighting credits to its BeamBrush 40 rig: Al Gurdon as show designer, Eric Marchwinski and Mark Humphrey as lighting directors and programmers, Harry Forster and Ben Green as lighting designers.243

Regular-season football has caught up. On December 28, 2025, the San Francisco 49ers ran an eight-minute "Bay Area Rock & Roll Laser Halftime Show" at Levi's Stadium during a game against the Chicago Bears, designed and produced by Image Engineering. The stadium's all-LED field lighting could switch between game and show almost instantly, and the team's Laura Johnson noted that the show "doesn't require anyone to be on the field".285

Does Disney still use lasers in its nighttime shows?#

At Disneyland Paris, yes, and with secondhand lasers of some pedigree. At the Paris 2024 opening ceremony on July 26, the Eiffel Tower carried "lasers and a custom spotlight package".247 In January 2025, AP reported that "Disney Tales of Magic" reuses lasers from the tower that night. The 20-minute show adds drones, fountains and projection, Tim Lutkin is artistic director, and AP says the beams "travel 23 miles (37 km)".286

What were the biggest New Year's Eve laser shows?#

Dubai's, by the official measure. For "Light Up 2018", Emaar replaced the Burj Khalifa fireworks with a light and laser show of about seven minutes. Guinness certified it as the "largest light and sound show on a single building", covering 109,252 square meters, more than double the previous record set on Hong Kong's ICC tower in 2013.287 Five years later Emaar announced that its 2022-23 show would set "a new world record for the largest laser display". That was a pre-event announcement, with no laser count or supplier.288

The industry's own prize went somewhere colder. ILDA's 2026 Corporate Show award went to "New Years Eve Eskilstuna 2026", by Merlin Schaadt and Laserimage AB of Sweden.289 Where lasers have replaced fireworks outright, and why, is in /now/.

What is Vivid Sydney's laser show?#

"Laser Lightfall", over the water at Cockle Bay, billed as the first Vivid Sydney Laser Show and "Australia's most ambitious free outdoor laser spectacular". Destination NSW produced it, ER Productions from the UK made the laser show, and Sonar Music (Antony Partos and Jono Ma) wrote the score. It ran 23 nights from May 28 to June 19, 2026: seven-minute shows, four an hour from 6 p.m. to 11 p.m., with some nights given over to a "Sky Symphony" version with fireworks.290

How does a festival laser department work?#

The laser company sends its own Laser Safety Officer (LSO), usually the person at the controls. Roberta McHatton, an LSO writing for the Event Safety Alliance in 2024, puts the job in one line: "The operator is responsible for stopping laser emission whenever a hazard is present!"177

Her riskiest hour is setup, not the show, when crew are in places nobody can predict and the audience has not arrived. Her checklist covers projector mounts and clamps (including on lifts that vibrate), fixed bounce mirrors, emission indicators, interlocks, beam attenuators and labels, and anything reflective in the venue. Crews align at the lowest power, lock the zones and post warning signs while they work. Zoning, software limits that keep beams out of set areas, counts as an engineering control, and the operator still watches for balloons and crowd surfers. Her rule of thumb puts lasers at least 3 m above audience feet.177

In the UK, HSE's leaflet INDG224 puts the duty to assess and control the risk on the organizer, who is expected to check that a hired laser company is competent. It lists the engineering controls - enclosures, blanking plates, scan-failure systems, emergency stops - and describes display lasers as running from about 200 mW to 40 W and up, able to burn skin and start fires above roughly 500 mW.30 Who carries which legal role, country by country, is in /rules/. The rest of a festival's machinery is in how festivals work.

Do outdoor laser shows need FAA permission?#

In the US the FAA does not permit shows; it reviews them. Under Advisory Circular 70-1B (2022), anyone sending visible beams above 50 nW/cm2 into navigable airspace, or any beam above the exposure limit, files Form 7140-1 at least 30 days ahead, with a Laser Configuration Worksheet for each setup and beam-path diagrams. The answer is a letter of determination: no objection, or objection.215 ILDA's one-page overview tells its members to file 90 days out, says review takes 30 to 90 days, and says the paperwork and the letter should be on site on show day.178

Near airports the brightness caps fall by zone, down to 50 nW/cm2 in the Laser-Free Zone. The circular's control measures include safety observers who can cut the beam, beam termination on surfaces, and radar or imaging; an automated system standing in for observers has to meet SAE AS6029.215 In the UK, HSE points organizers of outdoor shows to Civil Aviation Authority guidance and a NOTAM.30

Why do the beams go over the crowd in the US and into it in Europe?#

Because the default flips. ILDA's US overview says beams normally stay 3 m above the standing surface and 2.5 m to the side of it, unless the FDA has approved audience scanning in the show's variance.178 With that approval, it happens: the PASS units at Coachella 2025 were there for it.244

Europe is not one rule. In the UK the organizer carries the duty under general health and safety law, with PLASA's free, HSE-endorsed guidance standing in for the withdrawn HS(G)95, and the leaflet sets no numeric audience limit.30 The Nordic radiation safety authorities went the other way on June 20, 2017, jointly recommending that audiences not be exposed to high-power lasers at all. They counted diffraction gratings as nearly as risky, and where exposure happens anyway they want it below the eye limit, normally 2.55 mW/cm2 for a static beam or 1 mW through a 7 mm aperture, with continuous-wave lasers only, a measurement before every show at the worst spot, and everything written down.291 How a beam is kept under the limit is in /safety/.
