A laser guide star is an artificial star: a laser tuned to the sodium line excites sodium atoms in the mesosphere about 90 km up, and they glow.419 The telescope's adaptive optics measures how the atmosphere smears that point of light, 1000 times a second, and a deformable mirror reshapes itself to cancel the blur, giving images "as sharp as if the telescope were in space".419

At Paranal, ESO's Very Large Telescope Unit Telescope 4, Yepun, uses the 4 Laser Guide Star Facility, with four laser units of 22 W each; the test laser was 20 W (official ESO figures, 2018).419 ESO ordered the same TOPTICA-MPBC lasers for its Extremely Large Telescope.419

How does NASA talk to a spacecraft with a laser?#

Deep Space Optical Communications (DSOC) flew on the Psyche spacecraft, launched in 2023, and set up its first laser link with Earth about a month later.420 On December 11, 2023, it peaked at 267 Mbit/s, streaming ultra-high-definition video home.420 Its farthest link came on December 3, 2024, from 307 million miles; its final pass was from 218 million miles. Across 65 passes it brought down 13.6 terabits (official NASA figures).420

Aiming is the hard part. A 3 kW uplink laser at JPL's Table Mountain Facility sends a beacon so the spacecraft knows where to point its own laser back.420 Caltech's 200-inch telescope at Palomar was the main receiver, its detector array counting individual faint photons; a 1 m telescope at Table Mountain and a hybrid radio-optical antenna at Goldstone also caught the signal.420

How do the most extreme lasers reach petawatts?#

At ELI-NP in Magurele, Romania, the first 10 petawatt pulses fired in March 2019, after the system had held 7 PW for more than four hours.421 A petawatt is 10^15 watts, and it lasts only femtoseconds: ELI-NP's pulses are 25 fs long at 820 nm, focused to intensities up to 10^23 W/cm².421 Thales built the system and started installing it in late 2016.421 ELI has three sites - ELI-NP in Romania, ELI Beamlines in the Czech Republic and the ELI Attosecond Light Pulse Source in Hungary - and uses include physics under extreme conditions and accelerating electrons past 10 GeV.421

None of this works without chirped pulse amplification (CPA), published by Mourou and Strickland in 1985. CPA stretches a short pulse out in time, amplifies the longer and gentler version, then squeezes it back into an ultrashort, ultra-intense pulse.336 The stretch keeps the amplifier from destroying itself. CPA earned a share of the 2018 Nobel Prize in Physics and also underpins "millions of corrective eye surgeries" a year.336 Where the laser itself came from is in invention.

What are optical tweezers?#

A focused laser beam that can grab something. Arthur Ashkin's optical tweezers use the push of tightly focused light to hold and move particles, atoms, viruses and living cells.336 In 1987 he trapped living bacteria with them, unharmed.336 Ashkin shared the 2018 Nobel Prize in Physics, announced October 2, 2018, "for groundbreaking inventions in the field of laser physics", alongside Mourou and Strickland.336

Cue 17

Lasers in war, defense and space

The short version: The death ray arrived in fiction 62 years before the laser did, and the first lasers armies actually used did not burn anything: they measured distance and painted targets for bombs. Real laser weapons took decades and billions of dollars, and the US Air Force's flying version ended in an Arizona boneyard in 2012. The programs that finally reached ships and battlefields in the 2010s and 2020s are aimed mostly at drones, and their governments sell them on one number, the cost of a shot. Alongside them sit a 1995 treaty against blinding lasers, a run of diplomatic protests over lasers pointed at aircraft and ships, and a quieter use in orbit, where lasers carry data between satellites.

Was the laser invented as a death ray?#

The death ray came first. In 1898 H. G. Wells gave his Martians a heat-ray in The War of the Worlds, an "invisible, inevitable sword of heat" that a physicist writing in 2010 likened to an infrared laser beam.422

When Theodore Maiman made the first laser, a ruby rod at Hughes Research Laboratories in 1960, a newspaper headline called it a "death ray", which dismayed him.422 The invention itself is told under invention. The movies did not wait for the hardware. In 1964 Goldfinger strapped James Bond to a gold table with a laser cutting toward him, four years after the invention.422

The money had moved even earlier. The Pentagon was funding laser research before Maiman's result, driven by Cold War worries, yet lasers strong enough to work as weapons took about another 20 years to appear.422 In the meantime the lasers that first mattered in combat were instruments.

What did militaries actually build first?#

A laser rangefinder fires a short pulse of light at a target and times the echo; light's speed turns that time into a distance.

The second job changed air war. A target designator is a laser that marks a target with a spot of light, and a laser-guided bomb steers itself toward that spot.423 In 1968 the US Air Force ran a combat evaluation of the Texas Instruments BOLT-117, which the National Museum of the US Air Force calls the world's first laser-guided bomb; the Air Force then adopted the Paveway I series of guidance kits.423

The proof was a bridge. The Thanh Hoa "Dragon's Jaw" bridge in North Vietnam had survived repeated attacks since April 1965. Laser-guided bombs brought it down in 1972, along with the Paul Doumer Bridge near Hanoi, and then took out 100 more bridges in the war's last year.423 By the museum's count, almost half the laser-guided bombs dropped in Southeast Asia hit their targets, most of the rest landed within 25 feet, and the kits made a bomb 100 times more effective than a free-fall one.423

What was the Airborne Laser, and why did it end?#

The Airborne Laser was the death ray taken at its word: a Boeing 747 built to shoot down ballistic missiles as they rose. Formally the YAL-1A Airborne Laser Test Bed, it was a modified 747-400 Freighter carrying two solid-state lasers and a megawatt-class chemical oxygen iodine laser (COIL), tested at Edwards Air Force Base, California, from December 2002.424

It worked, at least on test day. On 3 February 2010 it destroyed a boosting solid-fuel test rocket launched from San Nicolas Island, California, and about eight days later it destroyed a liquid-fuel test missile.424 Boost phase, the powered climb right after launch, was the moment the aircraft was designed to engage.

The money was already leaving. After the Missile Defense Agency cut funding, the program became a science and technology effort in 2010.424 The Defense Department had put more than $5 billion into the aircraft (reported, 2012), and the debate around it centered on "its operational concept, cost, and employment limitations".425 The department ended experiments with the test bed, and on 14 February 2012 the jet made its last ferry flight to storage at Davis-Monthan Air Force Base in Arizona, the Boneyard.424425

What are the US Navy's LaWS and HELIOS?#

In 2014 the US Navy put a laser weapon on a working ship in the Gulf: LaWS, the Laser Weapon System. The Office of Naval Research announced on 10 December 2014 that it had operated aboard USS Ponce in the Persian Gulf from September to November that year.426 In testing it hit targets on a fast small boat, a ScanEagle drone and other moving targets.426 The Navy described a ladder of effects from non-lethal dazzle up to destruction "if necessary", and Chief of Naval Research Rear Adm. Matthew Klunder put the cost at "less than a dollar per shot" (official claim, 2014).426

LaWS was a prototype bolted onto an old ship. HELIOS was meant to be part of the ship. The name stands for High-Energy Laser with Integrated Optical Dazzler and Surveillance; Lockheed Martin built it, and it is described as a 60-plus-kilowatt class system (reported, 2025).427 Lockheed delivered it in August 2022 as the first tactical laser installed on an existing ship, the destroyer USS Preble.427

Its first shot at a drone surfaced in a report rather than a press release. The Pentagon's Director, Operational Test and Evaluation said in a report published in January 2025 that Preble had fired HELIOS at an aerial drone during fiscal 2024 testing, with no date or place given.427 Preble had moved to Yokosuka, Japan, arriving on 12 October 2024.427

Did Iron Beam make the first combat laser interceptions?#

Israel made the first public claim of lasers downing targets in combat, and the name on it is still argued over.

Around 27-28 May 2025 the Israeli military said high-power lasers had brought down about 40 drones during the wider war, most of them Hezbollah drones on the northern border, operated by a new Israeli Air Force tactical air defense battalion.428 Defense News reported it as Israel's claim and "a first in a combat engagement".428

Officials did not name the system. Released images showed Rafael's Lite Beam, officials pointed to Iron Beam-M, and the Ministry of Defense said no Iron Beam components were involved.428

Iron Beam itself arrived months later. The Times of Israel reported it was declared operational in September 2025 after final tests, and the Ministry of Defense handed the first combat-ready system to the IDF, announced on 28 December 2025.429 Rafael Advanced Defense Systems developed it and Elbit Systems supplies the laser.429 Its job is the small end of the threat list - rockets, mortar shells and drones - while David's Sling and Arrow handle larger ones, and officials pitch it on improving "the cost balance" against expensive interceptor missiles.429

What is DragonFire, and who else is building laser weapons?#

The UK's DragonFire went from trials to a shipbuilding date in one announcement. On 20 November 2025 the Ministry of Defence said it had shot down drones flying at up to 650 km/h at its range in the Hebrides, a UK first for tracking and downing such targets above the horizon.430 A GBP 316 million contract went to MBDA UK, and the system is due on a Royal Navy Type 45 destroyer by 2027, about five years earlier than first planned.430 The ministry put a shot at about GBP 10, against hundreds of thousands for a missile (official, 2025).430

Other governments describe their programs in their own terms.

Country Program What was reported Status of the claim
United States LaWS, USS Ponce Operated in the Persian Gulf, Sept-Nov 2014; small boat and ScanEagle targets426 Official (US Navy)
United States HELIOS, USS Preble 60-plus kW class; fired at a drone in FY2024 testing427 Reported (DOT&E via Military Times)
Israel Iron Beam; Lite Beam / Iron Beam-M About 40 drones downed in 2025; which system did it is disputed428 Official claim, reported
United Kingdom DragonFire 650 km/h drones downed, 2025; on a Type 45 by 2027430 Official (MOD)
Germany Naval laser demonstrator, frigate Sachsen Installed June 2022; six trial campaigns, more than 100 firings, drones downed431 Company statement, 2023
India Laser Directed Energy Weapon Mk-II(A) 30 kW class, vehicle-mounted; shown against a fixed-wing drone and swarm drones, 13 April 2025432 Official (DRDO)
China LY-1 Shown on trucks at the Beijing parade, 3 Sept 2025, as ship-based air defense433 Reported; no official power figure in the report
Russia Peresvet Announced by President Putin in March 2018; on combat duty from 1 Dec 2018, per Krasnaya Zvezda434 Official claim; purpose not stated

Germany's demonstrator was built by an MBDA Deutschland and Rheinmetall working group for the federal procurement office BAAINBw, with observers from the British, Dutch and Norwegian navies, and an operational system is named as the next phase with no date.431 DRDO chief Samir V. Kamat called India's demonstration at Kurnool, Andhra Pradesh, "just the beginning of the journey".432 For China's LY-1, CBS quoted analyst Alexander Neill telling the BBC it is believed able to disable electronics or blind pilots.433 Putin listed Peresvet among six major new weapons; no official purpose was given, and one expert told the press it could hide intercontinental missile deployment areas.434

Why are lasers aimed at drones first?#

Look down the list of recent programs and almost every one was first shown against drones: a ScanEagle for LaWS in 2014, a drone for HELIOS in fiscal 2024, about 40 drones in Israel's 2025 claim, 650 km/h drones for DragonFire, drones for Germany's frigate trials and swarm drones for India's Mk-II(A).426427428430431432

The pitch is arithmetic. Cost per shot is the operating cost of one laser engagement, and governments lead with it: under a dollar for the US Navy in 2014, about GBP 10 for the UK MOD in 2025, set against hundreds of thousands of pounds for a missile.426430 Israeli officials describe the same thing as improving "the cost balance".429

A cheap drone shot down by an expensive missile is a trade the defender loses even when the missile hits, and an attacker with many drones can keep making that trade. A laser that runs on a ship's or a truck's electrical power changes the price of each answer. Those per-shot figures are the governments' own claims and leave out what the systems cost to buy, which DragonFire's GBP 316 million contract hints at.430

What is a laser dazzler?#

A dazzler is a visible laser meant to make someone squint and turn away, not to hurt them. The Pentagon's Joint Intermediate Force Capabilities Office calls them optical distracters: visible lasers that produce temporary, reversible visual effects, used to hail and warn at vehicle checkpoints and for long-range "ocular interruption" at sea.435 The office says they are designed to operate below the maximum permissible exposure (MPE), the level treated as free of hazardous effects (exposure limits are explained under safety), and that they get legal reviews before fielding; it names the LA-9/P, GLARE MOUT and the Green Laser Interdiction System as fielded US examples.435

The vocabulary keeps moving. A later Defense Department fact sheet calls them ocular interrupters, classes them as non-blinding, and lists entry control points, checkpoints and convoy or vessel protection as their jobs; it names the Marine Corps LA-22 GLARE RECOIL and the Navy's Long Range Ocular Interrupter, which reached initial operational capability in fiscal 2024.436

The Navy has said it used one on a foreign crew. In December 2022 in the Strait of Hormuz, an IRGC Navy patrol boat came within 150 yards of USS Lewis B. Puller and USS The Sullivans and shone a spotlight at a bridge, and a Navy spokesman said The Sullivans answered with a handheld non-lethal laser dazzler.437

Are blinding laser weapons banned?#

Yes, by name. Protocol IV to the Convention on Certain Conventional Weapons, the Protocol on Blinding Laser Weapons, was adopted at the Vienna Review Conference, which ran from 25 September to 13 October 1995; the text is dated 13 October 1995.438

Article What it says
1 Bans using laser weapons specifically designed, as their sole combat function or one of them, to cause permanent blindness to unenhanced vision, and bans transferring them to any state or non-state entity438
2 Requires all feasible precautions, including training, to avoid permanent blindness when using any laser system438
3 Blinding as an incidental or collateral effect of legitimate military use of lasers, including against optical equipment, is not covered by the ban438
4 Defines permanent blindness as irreversible, uncorrectable, seriously disabling loss of vision: acuity below 20/200 Snellen in both eyes438

Unenhanced vision means the naked eye or eyes with ordinary corrective lenses.438

The ban in Article 1 turns on design and permanence: a weapon built to cause permanent blindness. Article 3 sets incidental or collateral blinding outside it, and Article 2's precautions apply to any laser system.438

The UN treaty record lists 111 parties, and the United States consented to be bound on January 21, 2009.439 The prosecutions and policy around it are in every kind of laser case.

Have governments accused each other of laser attacks?#

Several have, in formal protests. Each case below is the reporting government's account.

Djibouti, 2018. The Pentagon said US aircraft landing in Djibouti had been hit by lasers at least twice and possibly up to 10 times over a couple of weeks in April and May, that two Air Force C-130 crew members were slightly injured, and that the US had formally demarched China and asked it to investigate.440 A demarche is a formal diplomatic protest. Pentagon spokesperson Dana White: "Our concern is the safety of our service members."440

Arafura Sea, 2022. Australia's Department of Defence said that on 17 February a People's Liberation Army-Navy ship, one of two sailing east, had lit up a Royal Australian Air Force P-8A Poseidon with a laser, and called it "a serious safety incident".441 Lasing is the service term for shining a laser at an aircraft or ship.

Second Thomas Shoal, 2023. This one was a ship. The Philippine Department of Foreign Affairs said that on 6 February, near Ayungin (Second Thomas) Shoal, China Coast Guard vessel 5205 shadowed the Philippine Coast Guard ship BRP Malapascua and pointed a laser that temporarily blinded crew, and it lodged a formal protest on 14 February.442 The Philippine Coast Guard called the laser "military-grade".442

How are lasers used peacefully in space?#

The quietest military-adjacent laser story points up. Satellites are starting to talk to each other by laser, the same idea as fiber optics with the glass removed.

The biggest example on record is commercial. Reporting in February 2024 said the Starlink constellation, then more than 9,000 satellites, used inter-satellite laser links to move about 42 petabytes a day, roughly 42 million gigabytes (reported, 2024).443 The beams are infrared, and the figures come from secondary reporting of a SpaceX talk.443

Deep space is where the idea matters most: a narrow beam spreads less over a long distance than a radio signal, so more of it arrives.

NASA's laser link with the Psyche spacecraft is in big science.

The radio side of that sky, the signals laser links are starting to supplement, has its own guide at https://airwaves.cold.fyi/.

Cue 18

Why the FDA regulates lasers

The short version: In the United States a laser is legally an electronic product that gives off radiation, which puts it under the Food and Drug Administration. The FDA's performance standard sorts every laser into a class, says which labels and safety features each class needs, and holds pointers, toys and show projectors to low power unless the agency grants a variance. Enforcement runs mostly through paperwork and ports, while other countries reached pointers by a different road, treating the strong ones as weapons.

Why is the FDA in charge of lasers at all?#

The legal root is the Radiation Control for Health and Safety Act of 1968, Public Law 90-602.444 In 1990 the Safe Medical Devices Act moved that law into Chapter V of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act, where it now lives as sections 532, 534 and 542 (21 U.S.C. 360ii, 360kk and 360ss).444

The office that runs it is the FDA's Center for Devices and Radiological Health (CDRH).445 For lasers its work splits three ways: it writes and amends the performance standard, it issues guidance, including a numbered series of Laser Notices, and it acts on products, from repair and refund orders to holding shipments at the border.446447220

That makes it a regulator of products, not people. The FDA does not license laser users. It regulates what a laser is allowed to be when it leaves the factory, and, for shows, what it is allowed to do once someone switches it on. The forms, distances and audience rules for shows are in show laser rules.

What is in the laser performance standard?#

Two sections of federal regulation do most of the work. 21 CFR 1040.10 is the general standard every laser product must meet. 1040.11 adds rules for specific-purpose products, among them medical, surveying and demonstration laser products, the category that holds pointers and light shows.44428

The standard is older than most of the lasers it governs. FDA proposed it in 1974 (39 FR 32097) and published the final rule in 1975 (40 FR 32256), and by the agency's account in 2013 it was last updated in 1985.444

It works in four moves:

  • Classify. Every product is assigned a class by how much laser light it can emit. The classes run from I to IV.
  • Build to the class. Higher classes need more hardware. For a Class IIIb laser FDA lists a warning label, a key switch and a remote interlock connector, plus certification and identification labels and instructions for safe use.446
  • Certify. The manufacturer certifies that the product meets the standard and labels it to say so.446
  • Report. Projector makers file a Product Report (Form FDA 3632), show producers a Laser Light Show Report (Form FDA 3640), and both file annual reports.28
US class (21 CFR 1040.10) International name (IEC 60825-1) Visible output Where it shows up in the rules
IIIa 3R up to 5 mW (400-710 nm) The ceiling for laser pointers and for show projectors without a variance44628
IIIb 3B 5 to 500 mW May not legally be promoted as a pointer or demonstration laser446
IV 4 above Class IIIb Show projectors in this class need a variance28

The lower classes and what each means for an eye are in show safety.

A 2023 change lightened the paperwork for products built around someone else's laser. A final rule published January 20, 2023 and effective February 21, 2023 (Docket FDA-2018-N-3303) added conditions in 21 CFR 1010.2(e) under which a product that incorporates an already-certified laser does not need to be certified or reported again.448

What is a laser variance?#

A variance is FDA approval to depart from the standard, which the agency describes as "an alternate means of providing radiation safety."28 The best-known case is the light show. Show projectors are held to Class IIIa under 21 CFR 1040.11(c), so selling or operating Class IIIb or IV show equipment needs an approved variance, applied for on Form FDA 3147.28

The requirement reaches past the person at the console. FDA's list takes in light show producers, projector manufacturers, dealers and distributors, and users of permanent and temporary installations, and a firm may begin producing shows only after the approval letter arrives. Renewals follow Laser Notice 55.28 For beams going outdoors, the same page points to the FAA's Order 7400.2 on outdoor laser operations.28

The standard bends in other places too. The current rules let CDRH approve an "alternate means" of safety in place of a beam attenuator under 1040.10(f)(6), and the 2013 proposal would have let it approve alternate ways of providing certification and identification under 1010.2(d) and 1010.3(b).444

Why can't a laser pointer be stronger than 5 mW?#

Because a pointer is a demonstration product, and FDA regulation limits those to Class IIIa: 5 mW of visible light between 400 and 710 nm.446 FDA puts it flatly: "Class IIIb lasers cannot legally be promoted as laser pointers or demonstration laser products."446

The consequences FDA lists fall on the supply chain. A manufacturer can be required to repair, replace or refund, and imports can be detained or seized by Customs and Border Protection. Several states also require operators of Class IIIb lasers to register and pay annual fees.446

The agency's public warnings are blunter. "The FDA is aware of many child eye injuries caused when children play with laser pointers," it said in a December 2015 warning reported by Healio.449 A beam at or under 5 mW aimed into the eye can cause temporary flash blindness, FDA said, and one over 5 mW can cause irreversible eye injury. Its tips: don't buy pointers for children, and a pointer shown burning, melting or popping balloons is likely over 5 mW.449

What about toy lasers?#

Toys got their own guidance. In December 2014 CDRH issued the final guidance "Minimizing Risk for Children's Toy Laser Products" (Docket FDA-2012-D-1092), noting that the risk is "a particular concern for lasers intended for entertainment purposes."445

The harder line was in the 2013 proposed rule. It would have restricted lasers made or promoted as children's toys to Class 1 "under any conditions of operation, maintenance, service, or failure", with toys defined by expected use by children under 14.444 Class 1 is the bottom of the international scale, well below the 5 mW pointer ceiling.

How does FDA stop noncompliant lasers?#

Mostly at the dock. Import Alert 95-04 covers pointers, gunsights, laser pens, light show and special-effects projectors, levels, toy guns with lasers, key chains and similar devices.220 FDA's stated reason is that it "continues to encounter violations with foreign manufactured laser products".220

Detention without physical examination (DWPE) means a shipment from a firm on the alert's Red List can be held at the port on the listing alone. The problems FDA catalogs are mostly labels and paperwork: no permanent warning label, output over 5 mW, no certification or identification label, no safe-use instructions, a label and manual that disagree on class or output, and no product report on file.220 Importers can ask to recondition or relabel goods on Form FDA 766. The current version was published September 25, 2026, with guidance revised July 31, 2026.220 The Red List is long, and the alert itself is the place to read it.

Show gear has drawn FDA action too. On November 7, 2017, FDA issued a Safety Communication recommending against using projectors from two brands. It said units made on or after July 2, 2015 had not been sold under an approved variance, and some made from 2008 to July 1, 2015 might lack required features such as a manual reset and remote interlock connector.450 It was a product warning, not a warning letter, and no final resolution has been reported. The rest of that story is in every kind of laser case.

Is the US moving to the international standard?#

Slowly, and through guidance more than rules. The international laser standard is IEC 60825-1, and a product built to it has long faced a second set of requirements to sell in the US.

On June 24, 2013, FDA proposed fixing that (Docket FDA-2011-N-0070, RIN 0910-AF87). The proposal would have incorporated many provisions of IEC 60825-1:2007 and the medical laser standard IEC 60601-2-22:2007 by reference, so one set of rules would cover sales in the US and abroad.444 FDA would have kept its own limit on collateral x-ray emission and renamed the key control a "security master control". It would also have set the Class 1 limit for toys, written a 1976 Department of Defense exemption into the rule, and taken effect two years after a final rule.444

What came in 2023 was guidance, not a rule. Laser Notice 56, dated February 2023, says FDA "does not intend to enforce" the matching requirements of 1040.10 and 1040.11 for products that conform to the IEC 60825-1 Ed. 3 and IEC 60601-2-22 Ed. 3.1 clauses FDA has identified as comparable.447 Some IEC clauses "differ significantly", and those fall outside the notice.447 The same year's records-and-reports rule, above, trimmed paperwork but did not touch harmonization.448

Canada went further. Its 2024 rewrite, SOR/2024-196, replaced older rules for laser scanners and demonstration lasers with one broad standard built on IEC 60825-1 Ed. 3.0 (2014), in force a year after its October 9, 2024 publication.451

How do other countries handle laser pointers?#

Where the US caps pointers through product rules, several countries treat the strong ones as something closer to weapons. The common threshold is 1 mW, a fifth of the US ceiling.

Country Rule Threshold What it does
Australia (federal) Customs (Prohibited Imports) Regulations 1956, from July 1, 2008 above 1 mW Hand-held pointers treated as weapons for import452; the radiation agency calls anything above 1 mW "a prohibited weapon"453
Australia (New South Wales) Permit required from July 18, 2008 above 1 mW Having one in a public place without reasonable excuse is an offense; members of approved astronomy groups exempt454
New Zealand Health (High-power Laser Pointers) Regulations 2013, from March 1, 2014; Customs Import Prohibition Order 2019; Summary Offences Act 1981 above 1 mW Sale only by authorized suppliers to authorized recipients; import restricted; possession in public without reasonable excuse an offense455
Canada (aviation) Transport Canada rules above 1 mW No possession outside a private home in greater Montreal, Toronto or Vancouver, or within 10 km of an airport or certified heliport; work, school and astronomy uses exempt230
Canada (products) Canada Consumer Product Safety Act Class 3B or 4 Hand-held consumer pointers in these classes treated as a danger, so they cannot be sold, imported, advertised or manufactured451
United Kingdom Laser Misuse (Vehicles) Act 2018, in force July 10, 2018 no power limit Offense to shine a laser at a vehicle that is moving or ready to move where it dazzles or distracts, or is likely to, the person in control456

The UK law is about the target rather than the product. It covers trains, planes, cars and boats, extends protection to air traffic controllers, and drops the need to prove intent to endanger; the maximum is five years, an unlimited fine or both.457 Canada's new product standard sets no class cap on pointers at all; its ban sits in the consumer safety law instead.451

Do pointer bans work?#

The evidence is mixed. Australia brought in its import ban after "an increase in the number of incidents" from late 2007, and a 2012 government review found no significant drop in incidents after it took effect.452

Canada's case for its 2024 rules leaned on its own numbers. A 2018 Health Canada survey reported 318 laser eye injuries from 2013 to 2017, rising 34.4% a year, and of 581 hand-held lasers Health Canada tested from 2011 to 2023, 86% were mislabeled, unlabeled or incompletely labeled (official figures).451 The government put the cost to industry at $9 million undiscounted over 10 years (estimate).451

A pointer's label is often the only thing telling a buyer what is inside it, and in Canada's testing the label was usually wrong.

Cue 19

Every kind of laser case

The short version: Most laser cases in court are about aircraft: aiming a laser pointer at a plane has been a federal crime in the US since 2012, and the FAA took more than 10,000 reports a year from 2023 to 2025. The record sentence, 14 years, came down to five after an appeal. Beyond the cockpit the docket gets thin and strange, with malpractice studies, a ruling on police laser speed guns, a treaty against blinding weapons and one reported settlement after a nightclub injury in Spain.

Is it a crime to shine a laser at a plane?#

In the US, yes, under two separate systems. The criminal one is 18 U.S.C. 39A, added by the FAA Modernization and Reform Act of 2012 (Public Law 112-95, section 311) on February 14, 2012. It makes it a crime to knowingly aim the beam of a laser pointer at an aircraft, or at its flight path, punishable by a fine, up to five years in prison, or both.223 The FAA puts the criminal maximum at five years and a $250,000 fine.458 The exceptions cover authorized research, development and flight testing, Defense and Homeland Security duties, and emergency distress signals.223

The civil one is the FAA's own. Relying on 14 CFR 91.11, the rule against interfering with a crewmember (applied to lasers in a June 1, 2011 legal interpretation), the agency can fine up to $11,000 per violation and has imposed up to $30,800 for multiple incidents.225 The FAA tells police that state and local agencies are often in the best position to investigate a strike.459

The volume is why all this exists. Reports to the FAA peaked at 13,304 in 2023, then ran 12,840 in 2024 and 10,993 in 2025 (official); the agency's table showed 5,997 for 2026 as of its October 2026 update, with the year still under way.225 By March 2025 the FAA counted 328 injuries since it began tracking reports in 2010, and California (1,489) and Texas (1,463) led the 2024 count.458 The year-by-year table and how outdoor shows coordinate with the FAA are in show laser rules.

What was the longest sentence for lasering an aircraft?#

Fourteen years, for a while. A federal jury in Fresno convicted Sergio Patrick Rodriguez in December 2013, and on March 10, 2014 he was sentenced to 14 years for aiming a laser pointer at a Fresno police helicopter in 2012. The helicopter had gone up to investigate reported strikes on an emergency transport helicopter for Children's Hospital of Central California.460 Prosecutors said it was believed to be the longest sentence in a laser-strike case and that the laser was 13 times more powerful than the permitted level for hand-held devices.460 "This is not a game. It is dangerous, and it is a felony," said US Attorney Benjamin B. Wagner. The same jury convicted his co-defendant, Jennifer Lorraine Coleman.460

The appeal is where the case became law. In United States v. Rodriguez, 790 F.3d 951 (9th Cir. 2015), the Ninth Circuit vacated his conviction and sentence on the interference count under 18 U.S.C. 32(a)(5) and (a)(8), and sent the 39A count back for resentencing.461 Courthouse News reported the reason as insufficient evidence that he wanted to bring the helicopter down, and that aiming at the helicopter just once would have been enough for a 39A offense.462

On resentencing he got 60 months, the 39A maximum, against an advisory guidelines range of 21 to 27 months. The Ninth Circuit affirmed on October 25, 2016 in an unpublished memorandum (No. 15-10495), pointing to six or seven strikes, a serious criminal history and the fact that he committed the offense while on probation.461 Courthouse News reported that Coleman was sentenced in 2014 to two years in prison, a sentence later revoked and replaced with five years' probation.462

What sentences do people get now?#

Far less than 14 years, and spread wide. Recent federal cases, as the US Attorneys' offices describe them:

Defendant (district) What happened Plea Sentence
Nolan Wayne Hamman (D. Mont.) Lasered a plane flown by an instructor carrying a minor student, November 25, 2023 Guilty, October 2024 2.5 years, plus 3 years supervised release463
Sandra Roberson (W.D. Tex.) Struck a San Antonio Police Department helicopter, November 3, 2023 Guilty 37 months464
Christopher W. Harris (W.D. Wash.) Two counts, for lasering two small planes on November 20, 2022; one pilot's eyes were damaged Not stated in the release 8 months, plus 3 years supervised release with addiction and mental health treatment465
Glenwood Arthur Bringle (D. Ariz.) Aimed a laser at two US Air Force F-16s Guilty, February 26, 2025 9 days, plus 3 years supervised release, June 25, 2025466
Sergio Octavio Sanchez (D. Nev.) Aimed a green laser pointer at a Las Vegas Metropolitan Police Department helicopter, January 2, 2025 Guilty, one count 9 months, plus 3 years supervised release; the government had asked for 21 months467

How do other countries prosecute laser strikes?#

The UK charges them under the Laser Misuse (Vehicles) Act 2018, which reaches planes, trains, cars and boats and does not require proof of intent to endanger.457 On indictment the maximum is five years, a fine or both; the defenses are a reasonable excuse, or no intent plus all due diligence and reasonable precautions.456

Two recent cases, as police reported them. At Taunton Crown Court, Andrew Wilson admitted two counts under the Act, for lasering a helicopter over Yeovil on August 3, 2022 and an Airbus A320 descending toward Bristol Airport on August 14, 2022, and was sentenced on April 11 to four months' imprisonment, Avon and Somerset Police reported in April 2024.468 In Northern Ireland, Melvin Joyce pleaded guilty to shining a laser toward a moving helicopter and to recklessly or negligently endangering an aircraft, and was sentenced to four months in custody for the laser offense; the police release gives no year.469

Canada stacks its penalties. Possessing a hand-held laser over 1 mW in the restricted zones can bring a $1,000 fine under the Contraventions Regulations, or up to $5,000 for an individual and $25,000 for a corporation under the Canadian Aviation Regulations. Intentionally interfering with an aircraft by laser under the Aeronautics Act carries up to $100,000, five years, or both (Canadian dollars).230 Canada recorded nearly 250 reported laser strikes on aircraft in 2019 and 1,965 from 2015 to 2019 (official).230

Australia went after the supply instead: pointers over 1 mW have been prohibited imports since 2008. The rules country by country are in why the FDA regulates lasers.452

Has anyone sued over a concert laser?#

No US court record shows one. The cases that exist are overseas, and most are known second-hand through ILDA, the show laser trade association, which keeps a public incident list.470

Year Where What was reported Outcome on record
2008 Aquamarine Open Air, near Moscow 35 attendees went to hospitals and clinics with eye problems (ILDA, citing Reuters); the Moscow Health Department reported 12 patients with laser retinopathy No legal outcome found470204
2009 Tomorrowland, Belgium Eye injuries Authorities concluded they came from audience laser pointers, not the show470
2014 Spain A 29-year-old nightclub patron lost sight in one eye after a laser aimed at a mirror ball reflected into the audience €80,000 settlement reported (ILDA, citing El Periódico)470
2022, 2023 Kolhapur and Pune, India Vision loss reported in 65 people and 2 people after exposure to laser lights ILDA believes pulsed lasers were used470
April 2026 South Africa A misaimed festival laser hit the audience for about 30 seconds; one attendee was diagnosed with photokeratitis and no retinal injury (ILDA's account) None reported470

The trade's own count for regular shows is small. ILDA estimates that documented injuries from continuous-wave visible show lasers "appear to be in the single digits" against about 140 million people and 14 billion exposures; pulsed lasers and audience pointers are outside that count (ILDA estimate).471 The medical detail on Aquamarine and other show injuries is in show safety.

Phones and cameras are a liability question with no court answer yet. ILDA's position is that "laser show producers cannot be responsible" for damage to audience cameras, and it suggests settling with the laser operator in advance who is responsible.206 It mentions a 2019 claim that a lidar unit at CES damaged a camera sensor, with no outcome given.206

What happens to companies that sell noncompliant lasers?#

The product cases are regulatory, not courtroom. In the US the main tool is Import Alert 95-04, which lets ports detain listed firms' laser pointers, projectors, gunsights and toys without examining them, over missing labels, output above 5 mW and missing reports.220 FDA's November 7, 2017 Safety Communication on show projectors recommended against using two brands, saying newer units had not been sold under an approved variance and some older ones might lack a manual reset and remote interlock connector. ILDA quotes FDA calling shows with the noncompliant units "potentially dangerous" and reports that such shows are prohibited under federal law. One maker answered three days later that it had hired third-party consultants to certify each product, and ILDA reports no final resolution.450

Canada uses consumer product law: hand-held pointers in Class 3B or 4 are treated as a danger and cannot be sold, imported, advertised or manufactured.451

What do laser malpractice cases look like?#

Medicine supplies the money cases, and the journals have counted them. A JAMA Dermatology study published online October 16, 2013 found 175 injury cases from cutaneous laser procedures between 1999 and 2012. Non-physicians operated in 42.9% of them, a share that rose from 36.3% in 2008 to 77.8% in 2011, and laser hair removal was the most common procedure.472

An update in Dermatologic Surgery (March 2022) covered 69 claims from 2012 to 2020. Non-physicians were the operators in 71%, hair removal was 64% of claims, burns appeared in 77% and scarring in 39%.385 Of 36 cases with a known outcome, 53% went for the plaintiff, and indemnity payments ran from $1,665 to $1.5 million, with a mean of $320,975 and a median of $132,108 (reported, nominal dollars).385

Eye surgery is a different pattern. A 2017 study in Cornea searched WestlawNext and found 167 refractive surgery cases, 127 of them LASIK.473 Of those, 35.3% ended unfavorably for the defendant, as did 23.6% of the 141 jury trials, and 49.7% alleged lack of informed consent. The inflation-adjusted mean award was $1,287,872; verdicts averaged $1,604,801 and settlements $826,883. Keratoconus after surgery and the need for further surgery made a plaintiff win more likely.473 How the treatments work is in lasers in medicine.

How have courts treated police laser speed guns?#

As reliable, with conditions. In a March 20, 1998 ruling, a New Jersey Superior Court judge in Morris County held that the LTI Marksman 20-20 laser speed detector produced "reasonably uniform and reasonably reliable measurements".474 Readings came in without expert testimony if the officer's training and the maker's pre-operational checks were shown, by day or night, in light or moderately heavy rain, up to 1,000 feet. Heavy rain, falling snow or longer range meant an expert was needed.474

The court's testing put a number on the error: the laser read more than 1 mph above the comparison device in 16 of 1,908 cases.474 The ruling finished a matter that opened with a June 1996 opinion, in which the same judge accepted laser speed measurement in principle but found the device not yet shown reliable enough.475474

Who won the fight over inventing the laser?#

The patent wars over the laser itself, including Gordon Gould's decades of litigation, belong to the story of who invented it.

Is there a treaty against blinding lasers?#

Yes. Protocol IV to the Convention on Certain Conventional Weapons, on blinding laser weapons, was adopted in Vienna on October 13, 1995 and entered into force on July 30, 1998.439 The UN treaty record lists 111 parties, and the United States consented to be bound on January 21, 2009, with an understanding on Article 2.439 What the protocol bans and what it leaves alone is in lasers in war, defense and space.

Where can I see every laser case at once?#

The full table also lives at the docket.

What laser cases are missing from the record?#

No FDA enforcement action against a named festival, show company or venue appears in an FDA primary source; the nearest thing is the 2017 Safety Communication on two projector brands, a product action rather than one against a show.450 No US lawsuit over a concert laser injury has turned up in court records, and the one reported settlement, in Spain, is known second-hand.470 No court has ruled on who pays when a show laser kills a phone camera.206 The FAA publishes its civil penalty ceilings but not how many penalties it has brought.225

Cue 20

Test yourself, and where to dig next

The short version: A quiz on everything above, the dates in order, the landmark shows, the vocabulary, the best places to keep reading, and the questions nobody has answered in public yet.

Timeline

  3. 1954-04The maser gets its name Townes names the ammonia maser built at Columbia with Gordon and Zeiger. source

  7. 1960Lasers enter manufacturing TWI dates industrial laser use to the 1960s. source

  19. 1964-02-14Argon-ion laser William Bridges sees the 488 nm blue line at Hughes Research Labs. source

  21. 1966Leon Goldman's first laser surgery The dermatologist later called the father of laser surgery performs his first laser operation. source
  22. 1967Rockne Krebs shows laser beams as sculpture "Sculpture Minus Object" at the Washington Gallery of Modern Art used red beams, fog and mirrors. source
  23. 1967Rockne Krebs shows laser sculpture "Sculpture Minus Object" in Washington uses red beams, fog and mirrors. Other accounts date his first laser work to 1968. source
  24. 1968First laser-guided bomb The US Air Force combat-evaluates the Texas Instruments BOLT-117. source
  25. 1968Radiation Control for Health and Safety Act Public Law 90-602 puts radiation-emitting products, lasers included, under federal control. source
  26. 1969First lunar retroreflector Apollo 11 leaves a panel of 100 corner cubes on the Moon for laser ranging. source
  27. 1969-05-09Audio/Video/Laser at Mills College Lowell Cross, David Tudor and Carson Jeffries stage what Cross calls the first multi-color laser light show with x-y scanning. source
  28. 1969-09Krebs files a laser art patent US patent 3,622,228, which ILDA calls apparently the first patent for laser artistic display. source
  29. 1970First excimer laser A xenon dimer laser runs at the Lebedev Institute in Moscow. source
  30. 1970Argon photocoagulator goes on sale Coherent begins marketing a slit-lamp argon laser for the retina. source
  31. 1970-03-18Pepsi Pavilion opens at Expo '70 A four-color krypton laser system by Cross and Jeffries runs in Osaka; Cross gives the dedication date as March 15. source
  32. 1971Laser printing at PARC Starkweather builds SLOT on a Xerox 7000 copier; laser printing is shown at one page per second. source
  33. 1972Dragon's Jaw falls Laser-guided bombs bring down the Thanh Hoa and Paul Doumer bridges. source
  34. 1972-08-01New York regulates lasers Industrial Code Rule 50 takes effect, ahead of the US federal laser standard. source
  35. 1973-11-19Laserium opens at Griffith Observatory Ivan Dryer's laser show opens in Los Angeles and runs there for nearly three decades. source
  36. 1973-11-19Laserium opens at Griffith Observatory Ivan Dryer's live-played laser show begins a trial run in Los Angeles and stays 28 years. source
  37. 1974-06-26First UPC scan A pack of Juicy Fruit gum is scanned by a Spectra-Physics helium-neon scanner in Troy, Ohio. source
  38. 1975US laser performance standard published FDA's final rule (40 FR 32256), proposed in 1974, creates the standard now at 21 CFR 1040.10 and 1040.11. source
  39. 1975-02-13Led Zeppelin photographed with lasers Three beams at Nassau Coliseum, New York - the earliest dated evidence of lasers at a rock show. source
  40. 1975-10-18The Who's argon laser at Leicester A 4 W argon laser fanned through a diffraction grating at Granby Halls. source
  41. 1976Moving-magnet galvo patented The design became practical with neodymium magnets in the late 1980s and reached production in 1992. source
  42. 1977Xerox 9700 Xerox launches the 9700 laser printer. source
  43. 1977FDA Laser Notice 22 Light shows fall under the federal standard and show producers count as manufacturers. FDA pages date it November 22 or 23. source
  44. 1977-02-04Lovelight, the first automated show A planetarium laser show run from stored images and recorded control tracks, with no live player. source
  45. 1977-02-04"Lovelight" runs from stored images Described by LaserFX as the first automated laser show: images drawn on a digitizing pad and stored in PROMs, control tracks on an 8-track recorder. source
  46. 1977-10-11Gould wins his first basic laser patent US 4,053,845 on optically pumped laser amplifiers issues after a long fight. source
  47. 1978"Icarus" on steam screens Laser images projected onto steam screens over Washington DC in summer 1978. source
  48. 1978-07-10Time reports FDA inspectors at light shows The Bureau of Radiological Health had told Blue Oyster Cult to adjust its show and halted a preview on the Mall in Washington. source
  49. 1981Metal-ceramic ion tubes Coherent introduces Innova tubes, lifting tube life from about 1,500 hours toward 5,000. source
  50. 1981-10-21Jarre plays the laser harp in China Five concerts in Beijing and Shanghai, on a harp ordered from Bernard Szajner; 218,000 attended by the artist's figure. source
  51. 1981-11The turkey experiment IBM's Srinivasan, Wynne and Blum ablate turkey cartilage with an excimer laser. source
  52. 1983Stone Mountain laser show opens The Georgia park's mountain-side show begins; the park estimated 20 million viewers in its first 25 years. source
  53. 1983Selective photothermolysis Parrish and Anderson publish the idea in Science that makes targeted skin lasers possible. source
  54. 1983Stone Mountain Lasershow begins A mountain face in Georgia becomes a laser screen about 400 ft square. source
  55. 1985Chirped pulse amplification Mourou and Strickland publish the method behind petawatt lasers. source
  56. 1985-08-2021 CFR 1040.11(c) takes its current form The demonstration laser rule names Classes I, IIa, II and IIIa (50 FR 33702). source
  57. 1986Pangolin's first product, Laser PC The company's About page gives 1986 for its founding; another company page says 1985. source
  58. 1986-04-05Rendez-vous Houston Jarre uses downtown skyscrapers as screens; attendance is estimated at 1.3 million or 1.5 million. source

  60. 1986-08-16ILDA founded The International Laser Display Association holds its first organizational meeting in Lake Tahoe. source
  61. 1986-08-20US manual-reset rule Class IV laser systems made after this date must need a manual reset after an interlock trip or a power loss over 5 seconds. source
  62. 1987Optical tweezers hold bacteria Ashkin captures living bacteria with focused laser light without harming them. source
  63. 1988Gould's royalties begin His fourth basic laser patent is approved in April. source

  65. 1988-03First laser vision correction The first corrective laser eye surgery on a sighted person. source
  66. 1989First ILDA DB-25 specification Later superseded by the ISP-DB25 section of the ILDA Standard Projector. source

  72. 1995-10ILDA Test Pattern revision 002 The published standard sets tuning at 12,000 or 30,000 points per second. source
  73. 1995-10ILDA Test Pattern Rev. 002 The circle-in-a-square tuning frame, at 12,000 or 30,000 points per second. source
  74. 1995-10-13Blinding lasers banned Protocol IV to the CCW is adopted in Vienna. source
  75. 1995-10-13Protocol IV adopted Vienna adopts the protocol on blinding laser weapons; it enters into force on July 30, 1998. source
  76. 1996HSE publishes HS(G)95 UK guidance on display laser safety, published in October to replace note PM19 of 1980; since withdrawn. source

  78. 1998-07-30First LASIK laser approval FDA approves the Kremer Excimer Laser System (PMA P970005). IBM's history says 1997. source
  79. 1998-12-22O'Hagan thesis A Loughborough University PhD on entertainment laser risk finds audience scanning not acceptable as then practiced. source

  81. 1999-07ILDA Standard Projector Rev. 002 Clarified signal levels and interlock on the DB-25 connector. source

Everything a laser does

86 uses, by field

UseLaserWhat it does
Metal 3D printing (laser powder bed fusion)
Aerospace / manufacturing · since 2004 (GE LEAP nozzle development)		Melts metal powder layer by layer; GE LEAP fuel nozzle consolidated about 20 parts into one, 25% lighter source
Laser guide stars
astronomy		TOPTICA-MPBC · sodium line · 22 W per unit (VLT, 2018)Excites sodium about 90 km up to make an artificial star for adaptive optics. source
Battery busbar and cell welding
Automotive / e-mobility		Welds busbars joining cells into modules and packs (Al/Al, Cu/Cu, Al/Cu), seals cells gas-tight, welds trays and housings source
Copper contact welding (green laser)
Automotive / e-mobility		Green-wavelength disk laserMaker says it welds copper contacts with practically no spatter source
Hairpin stator and drivetrain welding
Automotive / e-mobility		Welds hairpin stators for e-motors, rotor shafts, gears and fuel-cell bipolar plates source
Optical tweezers
biology and physics · since 1987		Focused laser light holds and moves particles, atoms, viruses and living cells. source
Telephone call over a laser beam
communications · since 1960-12-13		helium-neon · 1.15 micrometersJavan's team carried a telephone call over the first helium-neon beam the day after it first ran. source
Laser pointers
consumer		 · FDA: do not buy over 5 mW (2023 guidance)Hand-held pointers; many tested above their labeled power. source
Laser toys and cat toys
consumer		 · FDA: toys within IEC Class 1 (2023 guidance)FDA lists laser cat toys as an example of laser toys. source

Consumer / hobby		Creality Falcon2 · 22 W, Class 4UK regulator found it excessively strong for consumer use and rejected the import at the border (2024) source

Consumer / hobby · since 2021 (xTool D1 launch)		Diode laser module (Bambu Lab H2D) · 455 nm and 850 nm · 10 W ± 1 W (maker spec)Class 4 module inside a machine rated Class 1 when its protection is complete and working source

consumer electronics		 · 405 nmMultilayer Blu-ray: 100 GB (3 layers), 128 GB (4 layers). source

Airborne missile-defense laser (Airborne Laser, YAL-1A)
Defense · since 2002		Chemical oxygen iodine laser (COIL) plus two solid-state lasers · Megawatt-class (official)A modified 747 tested against boosting missiles at Edwards AFB, 2002-2012; retired to storage in 2012. source

Defense · since 2025		High-power laser (system disputed: Lite Beam, Iron Beam-M)Israel said about 40 drones were downed in 2025; the Ministry of Defense said no Iron Beam components were involved. source

Defense · since 2025		High-power laser (Rafael; laser by Elbit Systems)Declared operational September 2025; first combat-ready system delivered to the IDF in December 2025. source
Laser target designation for guided bombs

Long-range naval ocular interrupter
Defense · since 2024		Visible laserUS Navy Long Range Ocular Interrupter reached initial operational capability in fiscal 2024. source
Naval counter-drone laser (DragonFire)
Defense · since 2025		UK MOD says it downed drones at up to 650 km/h; due on a Type 45 destroyer by 2027; about GBP 10 per shot (official claim, 2025). source

Defense · since 2022		Installed on the frigate Sachsen in June 2022; more than 100 test firings, drones downed (company statement, 2023). source
Non-lethal dazzler (optical distracter)
Defense		Visible laser · Designed below maximum permissible exposure (official)Temporary, reversible glare to hail and warn at checkpoints and at sea; fielded US examples include the LA-9/P and GLARE MOUT. source
Shipboard laser weapon (LaWS)
Defense · since 2014		US Navy prototype on USS Ponce, Persian Gulf, Sept-Nov 2014; small boat and ScanEagle drone targets; under $1 per shot (official claim, 2014). source

Caries removal and restorations
dentistry		FDA has cleared lasers for removing decay and tooth-colored restorations. source
Root canal adjunct
dentistry		Cleared as an adjunct; clinical benefit of laser disinfection not known (AAE, 2019). source
Soft tissue dental surgery
dentistry		FDA has cleared lasers for gum and other soft tissue work. source
Fractional resurfacing
dermatology · since 2000s		Fractional (Fraxel)Treats skin in columns with untreated skin between; FDA-cleared in the early 2000s. source
Hair removal
dermatology · since 1990s		RubyDeveloped by Anderson and Grossman; an expert calls it the most common laser treatment in medicine. source
Home hair-growth devices (LLLT)
dermatology · since 2007		Low-level red light · about 650 nm (620-678 nm)First FDA-cleared device for male pattern hair loss in 2007; 32 cleared home-use devices on the US market as of January 5, 2020. source
Port-wine stain treatment
dermatology · since 1989		Pulsed dyeTargets blood vessels to treat port-wine stains without scarring. source
Skin resurfacing

Tattoo and pigmented lesion removal
dermatology · since 2013		Picosecond (PicoSure)First picosecond laser for tattoos, FDA-cleared by January 2, 2013. source
Tattoo removal
dermatology		Q-switchedShort pulses break up tattoo ink. source

Displays		Excimer laser (line beam)Recrystallizes amorphous silicon into LTPS with up to 200x mobility for AMOLED phone displays (company claim) source
Inertial confinement fusion (NIF)
energy research · since 2022		192 beams · 2.05 MJ per ignition shot (2022)Laser-made x-rays implode a fuel capsule; first ignition December 5, 2022, 3.15 MJ out. source
Police lidar speed enforcement
law enforcement		Laser speed readings ruled admissible in Chicago Traffic Court on February 10, 2010. source
Drilling, soldering, heat treating, scribing, sealing, balancing
Manufacturing		Listed by OSHA among industrial laser applications source
Remote cutting
Manufacturing		A high-intensity beam vaporizes thin sheet with no assist gas source
Sheet metal cutting (CO2)
Manufacturing · since 1960s		CO2 laser · about 10.6 µm · 4-6 kW typical (reported, 2016)Melts or burns a kerf through steel, stainless and aluminum with nitrogen or oxygen assist gas; faster than fiber on material over 5 mm at equal power source

Laser cleaning and coating removal
Manufacturing / maintenance		Pulsed laserAblates paint, rubber, insulation or rust; cleans electronics contacts and tire molds; strips paint from bridges, aircraft and ships source

Medical devices		21 CFR 801.45 requires a permanent UDI on devices used more than once and reprocessed; the rule names no marking method source
Eye surgery with chirped pulse amplification
medicine		chirped pulse amplified ultrashort-pulse laserThe Nobel committee says chirped pulse amplification is used in millions of eye operations a year (2018). source
First medical laser treatment of a human patient
medicine · since 1961-12		The Photonics Spectra timeline records the first medical laser treatment of a human patient in December 1961. source

office · since 1971		A laser writes the page onto a xerographic copier process; Xerox 9700 launched 1977. source

ophthalmology · since 1995		Treats the eye's drainage tissue to lower pressure in glaucoma. source
SMILE
ophthalmology · since 2016		Femtosecond (Zeiss VisuMax)Cuts a disc inside the cornea removed through a small incision; FDA approval September 13, 2016. source
Chirped pulse amplification
physics · since 1985		Stretch, amplify and recompress a pulse for extreme peak power; also underpins corrective eye surgery. source
Gravitational wave detection (LIGO)
physics · since 2015		 · 200 W (after 2010-2015 upgrade, from 10 W)Michelson interferometers with 4 km arms detected a black hole merger on September 14, 2015. source
Laser cooling and trapping
physics · since 1987		Laser light slows atoms to microkelvin temperatures; magneto-optical trap built 1987. source
Petawatt research lasers (ELI-NP)
physics · since 2019		 · 820 nm · 10 PW peak, 25 fs pulses (2019)Physics under extreme conditions and accelerating electrons past 10 GeV. source
Supermarket barcode scanner
retail · since 1974		helium-neonA beam off a rotating mirror sweeps the barcode; a photodiode reads the reflection. source
Attosecond pulses for studying electrons
science · since 2001		Pulses of 250 attoseconds (trains) and 650 attoseconds (single) used to study electron dynamics in matter. source
Laser cooling and trapping of atoms
science		Methods to cool and trap atoms with laser light won the 1997 Nobel Prize in Physics. source
Laser holography
science · since 1963		Leith and Upatnieks at the University of Michigan made the first laser holograms in late 1963. source
Laser precision spectroscopy and the frequency comb
science		Recognized with the 2005 Nobel Prize in Physics to Hall and Hänsch. source
Laser spectroscopy
science		Recognized with the 1981 Nobel Prize in Physics to Bloembergen and Schawlow. source
Optical tweezers
science · since 1987		A focused laser beam holds tiny particles; Ashkin used tweezers to hold living bacteria in 1987. source

Semiconductors		Pulsed CO2 drive laser · 13.5 nm EUV output · 40 kW (product page); above 30 kW mean (2018)Pre-pulse flattens 25 µm tin droplets and a main pulse turns them into EUV-emitting plasma 50,000 times a second source
