transport · since 2020		Waymo 5th-generation 360-degree roof lidar sees objects beyond 300 m (company figure), plus four perimeter lidars. source

Glossary

230 terms of art

  • 14 CFR 91.11 The FAA rule against interfering with a crewmember, which the agency uses for civil penalties in laser cases. read more
  • 18 U.S.C. 39A The US federal crime of knowingly aiming a laser pointer at an aircraft or its flight path, punishable by up to five years in prison and a fine. read more
  • 510(k) clearance FDA permission to market a device because it is substantially equivalent to one already on the market. read more
  • Abstracts Non-representational moving laser patterns. read more
  • Adaptive optics A deformable mirror that reshapes itself about 1000 times a second to cancel atmospheric blur. read more
  • Aerial marker Lasers set up to throw beams into the sky as a landmark, as at Coachella's Quasar stage in 2025. read more
  • AIDC Automatic identification and data capture, such as a 2D code. read more
  • Airborne Laser (YAL-1) US Boeing 747-based missile-defense laser test aircraft, tested at Edwards Air Force Base from 2002 and retired to storage in 2012. read more
  • Analog modulation Continuous brightness control of each laser, usually 256 steps per color. read more
  • Anchor point Repeated points at a line end or corner that give the scanners time to stop or turn. read more
  • ANSI Z136.1 The US voluntary consensus standard for safe laser use, which OSHA cites as guidance. read more
  • Argon-ion laser A gas laser with strong blue and green lines, first seen working on February 14, 1964. The bright beam of 1970s rock tours. read more
  • Art-Net A network transport for DMX lighting control data. read more
  • Assignment for the Benefit of Creditors (ABC) A state-law alternative to bankruptcy, used by Glowforge in 2025. read more
  • Assist gas Gas blown down a cutting nozzle: oxygen adds heat by burning the metal, nitrogen only blows out the melt. read more
  • Audience scanning Beams deliberately swept through the crowd at levels meant to stay within the exposure limit. Excluded by the standard conditions of a US variance. read more
  • Automated show A laser show run from recorded control tracks, with nobody playing it. Lovelight (1977) is described as the first. read more
  • Aversion response The blink or turn-away reflex to bright light. Exposure limits for visible beams assume it takes 0.25 seconds. read more
  • Beam Attenuation Map (BAM) A 64 by 64 brightness map over a zone that dims or blocks output in chosen areas (Pangolin's term). read more
  • Beam show A show seen in mid-air: shapes scanned through haze as beams, fans, cones and tunnels. read more
  • Beam table A row of actuators that switch the beam to fixed positions, each aimed at a bounce mirror. read more
  • Beam termination Ending a beam on a surface so it goes no farther into the sky or the crowd. read more
  • BEYOND Pangolin's laser show control software, named in most festival case studies. read more
  • Blanking Switching the beam off while the scanners move between parts of an image. read more
  • Boost phase The early powered flight of a missile, when the Airborne Laser was designed to engage it. read more
  • Bureau of Radiological Health (BRH) The US Food and Drug Administration unit that inspected laser light shows in the late 1970s. read more
  • Busbar A metal link joining battery cells into modules and packs, often laser welded. read more
  • Busking Running cues live to the music instead of playing back a time-coded show. read more
  • CDRH The FDA's Center for Devices and Radiological Health, which receives laser product reports and variance applications. read more
  • Chirped pulse amplification Stretching a light pulse, amplifying it, then compressing it again; the subject of half the 2018 Nobel Prize in Physics. read more
  • Class 3B / IIIb Above 5 mW up to 500 mW. Hazardous even for brief accidental direct exposure. read more
  • Class 3R / IIIa Up to 5 mW of visible output; the most a US show projector may emit without an FDA variance. read more
  • Class 4 / IV Above 500 mW. An eye and skin hazard from the direct or reflected beam, and often a fire hazard. read more
  • Class IIIa (3R) Visible output up to 5 mW; the US ceiling for laser pointers and for show projectors without a variance. read more
  • Class IIIb (3B) Output from 5 to 500 mW; in the US it cannot legally be promoted as a laser pointer or demonstration laser. read more
  • Class IV (4) The class above IIIb; US show projectors in this class need an FDA variance. read more
  • Closed loop A scanner whose position detector feeds a servo amplifier that corrects the mirror. read more
  • Coherence Light waves staying in step in phase, because each stimulated photon copies the one that triggered it. read more
  • COIL Chemical oxygen iodine laser, the megawatt-class laser carried by the Airborne Laser. read more
  • Consulting physician (Texas) The doctor a Texas laser hair removal facility must have a written contract with. read more
  • Continuous wave (CW) Laser output that is steady over time rather than pulsed. read more
  • Copper-vapor laser A gas laser used on large 1990s tours; Pink Floyd's 1994 US tour carried two 50 W systems. read more
  • Corner cube retroreflector A three-mirror prism that sends light straight back where it came from; Apollo 11's panel holds 100 of them. read more
  • Cost per shot The operating cost of one laser engagement, the main argument governments make for laser weapons. read more
  • Critical Flight Zone FAA airspace within 10 NM of an airport up to 10,000 ft above ground, capped in Advisory Circular 70-1B at 5 microwatts/cm2. read more
  • DAC Digital-to-analog converter: the hardware that turns software output into the voltages a projector takes. read more
  • Dazzler (optical distracter, ocular interrupter) A visible laser meant to cause temporary, reversible glare, used as a warning at checkpoints and at sea. read more
  • Demarche A formal diplomatic protest. read more
  • Demonstration laser product The US legal term for a laser made, designed, intended or promoted for demonstration, entertainment, advertising display or artistic composition. read more
  • Dichroic A mirror or filter that passes one color and reflects others; used to merge red, green and blue into one beam. read more
  • Diffraction grating A ruled sheet that splits one beam into a fan of many. The Who's 1975 effect used a hand-held one. read more
  • Diffraction limit The smallest spot a lens can focus light into, set by the wavelength. read more
  • Direct diode A semiconductor chip that lases when current is applied to it, with no crystal in between. read more
  • Directed energy weapon A weapon that uses focused energy, such as a laser or microwaves, instead of a projectile. read more
  • Divergence How fast a beam widens as it travels. Doubling it halves the hazard distance. read more
  • DPSS Diode-pumped solid state: an 808 nm diode pumps a crystal lasing at 1064 nm, which a second crystal doubles to 532 nm green. read more
  • DragonFire UK laser weapon led by MBDA UK, due on a Royal Navy Type 45 destroyer by 2027. read more
  • DWPE Detention without physical examination: holding an import at the port because its firm is on an FDA import alert's list. read more
  • EDFA (erbium-doped fiber amplifier) A stretch of fiber laced with erbium and pumped by a laser at 980 or 1480 nm, which boosts 1550 nm signals without turning them into electronics. read more
  • Embedded laser The higher-class laser inside a Class 1 enclosed product; its class applies during service. read more
  • Energy level One of the fixed amounts of energy an atom or molecule can hold; the lowest is the ground state. read more
  • EUV Extreme ultraviolet lithography light at 13.5 nm, made by hitting tin droplets with a laser. read more
  • Excimer laser An ultraviolet gas laser that removes tissue in very thin layers with little heat; the laser behind PRK and LASIK. read more
  • Excimer laser annealing (ELA) Melting and recrystallizing amorphous silicon with an excimer line beam to make low-temperature polysilicon. read more
  • FB4 Pangolin's networked laser control hardware. read more
  • Femtosecond laser A laser whose pulses last quadrillionths of a second, used to cut corneal flaps or lenticules. read more
  • Fenning Award ILDA's award for technical achievement. read more
  • Fiber laser A solid-state laser whose gain medium is a doped optical fiber; it outsold CO2 for cutting from 2015. read more
  • Flame cutting Laser cutting with oxygen assist, which adds heat through an exothermic reaction. read more
  • Flicker Visible strobing when a frame has so many points that it is redrawn too few times a second. read more
  • Fog A thicker, billowing mist that shows swirls inside the beams. read more
  • Form 7140-1 The FAA's Notice of Proposed Outdoor Laser Operation(s), filed at least 30 days before an outdoor show. read more
  • Fractional resurfacing Treating skin in a grid of tiny columns, leaving untreated skin between them to speed healing. read more
  • Frame One complete list of points for one image; frames played in sequence make animation. read more
  • Frye hearing A US court hearing on whether a scientific method is generally accepted before evidence from it is admitted. read more
  • Fusion cutting Cutting by melting the metal and blowing the melt out with inert gas such as nitrogen. read more
  • Gain medium The material where amplification happens: a gas, crystal, dye, fiber or semiconductor. read more
  • Galvo Galvanometer scanner: a limited-rotation motor with a small mirror on its shaft. Two of them aim the beam. read more
  • Geometric correction Warping the output with sliders or a mesh so shapes land correctly on the target. read more
  • Grand rights Dramatic performance rights - music used to tell a story, as in opera or musical theater. They are negotiated directly with the rights holder and are outside a blanket license. read more
  • Graphics show A show in which the laser draws logos, text and animation on a surface. read more
  • Hairpin A bent copper bar in an e-motor stator, laser welded at its ends. read more
  • Haze A very fine, evenly spread mist that makes beams visible as smooth cones and sheets. read more
  • HELIOS High-Energy Laser with Integrated Optical Dazzler and Surveillance, a Lockheed Martin system delivered to USS Preble in 2022. read more
  • Helium-neon laser A low-power gas laser. Its red 632.8 nm line, found in 1962, gave the first continuous visible beam sold commercially. read more
  • High-energy laser Military term for a laser weapon meant to damage a target. read more
  • HIP Hot isostatic pressing, used after metal printing to close internal pores. read more
  • Hohlraum The centimeter-sized gold cylinder at NIF that turns laser light into x-rays to squeeze the fuel capsule. read more
  • Hologram A recording of the full wavefield of light that replays a true 3D image when lit by a laser. read more
  • IAPLC ILDA Accredited Professional Lasershow Company: an industry credential requiring documented shows, client references, a staff member who has passed a Laser Safety Officer course and a signed safety affirmation. read more
  • IDN ILDA Digital Network, a protocol for streaming laser graphics, effects and DMX data over a network. read more
  • IDTF (.ild) ILDA Image Data Transfer Format, for exchanging laser show frames and files between systems. read more
  • Ignition A fusion shot that releases more energy than the laser energy delivered to the target. read more
  • ILDA The International Laser Display Association, the laser show trade body. read more
  • ILDA Awards The laser display industry's annual prizes, with artistic categories such as Beams/Atmospherics, Graphics, Planetarium and Live Stage. read more
  • ILDA test pattern A standard frame, including a circle in a square, used to tune scanners and blanking at 12,000 or 30,000 points per second. read more
  • Import alert An FDA instruction that lets border staff detain listed firms' products without examining them; Import Alert 95-04 covers laser pointers and light show projectors. read more
  • Import Alert 95-04 FDA import alert that lets US ports detain listed firms' laser pointers, projectors, toys and similar products without physical examination. read more
  • INDG224 The UK HSE leaflet on display lasers, which puts the duty on the organizer and points to PLASA's guidance. read more
  • Inter-satellite laser link A laser beam carrying data between satellites. read more
  • Ion laser An argon or krypton gas laser; the professional show source before solid state, water-cooled and about 0.05% efficient. read more
  • Iron Beam Israel's high-power laser air defense, developed by Rafael with a laser from Elbit Systems; declared operational in 2025. read more
  • Irradiance Power spread over an area, in mW/cm2. It falls with distance as a beam widens. read more
  • ISP-DB25 (ILDA cable) The 25-pin analog connection carrying X, Y, color, shutter and interlock signals to a projector. read more
  • Kerf The slot a laser cut leaves. read more
  • Kinetic Field EDC Las Vegas's main stage. read more
  • kpps Thousands of points per second, the scanner speed rating. read more
  • Krypton-ion laser A gas laser that can give red, yellow, green and blue at once. Laserium split one krypton beam into four colors with prisms. read more
  • Lase-off Laserists showing off their work to each other. First recorded on August 14, 1986. read more
  • Laser Light amplification by stimulated emission of radiation; the acronym is first recorded in Gordon Gould's November 1957 notebook. read more
  • Laser cleaning Ablating a coating or surface layer with very brief high-power pulses, without chemicals or contact. read more
  • Laser emission source The unit Guinness counts for its largest laser show record: each point where a beam leaves a device, with a beam split by diffraction counted once. read more
  • Laser Floyd The usual name for a planetarium laser show set to Pink Floyd, most often Dark Side of the Moon or The Wall. Several companies have made their own. read more
  • Laser Free Zone FAA airspace closest to runways, where visible laser light is capped at 50 nW/cm2. read more
  • Laser guide star An artificial star made by a laser exciting sodium atoms about 90 km up, used to correct telescope blur. read more
  • Laser harp An instrument made of upright laser beams. Breaking a beam with the hand triggers a note. read more
  • Laser Jockey ILDA's live laser performance competition. read more
  • Laser mapping Laser outlines fitted to the features of a building or 3D object. read more
  • Laser Misuse (Vehicles) Act 2018 UK law making it an offense to shine a laser at a vehicle that is moving or ready to move where it dazzles or distracts the person in control. read more
  • Laser Notice A numbered FDA guidance letter to the laser industry, such as Notice 51 on projector sales or Notice 55 on variance renewal. read more
  • Laser Notice 56 FDA guidance (February 2023 version) saying the agency does not intend to enforce matching US requirements for products conforming to identified IEC 60825-1 Ed. 3 and IEC 60601-2-22 Ed. 3.1 clauses. read more
  • Laser Notice No. 56 FDA policy letting makers certify to parts of IEC 60825-1 Ed. 3; it replaced Notice 50 at the end of 2024. read more
  • Laser performance standard The US federal rule for laser products at 21 CFR 1040.10 and 1040.11, first published as a final rule in 1975. read more
  • Laser pointer maculopathy Damage to the center of the retina from looking into a handheld laser. read more
  • Laser powder bed fusion Building a metal part by melting thin layers of powder with a laser; also called DMLM, DMLS or SLM. read more
  • Laser retinopathy Damage to the retina from laser exposure. read more
  • Laser Safety Officer (LSO) The named person responsible for laser safety at a facility or show. Required by name in several US states and in Germany. read more
  • Laser-Free Zone The FAA flight zone near runways where outdoor laser light must stay at or below 50 nW/cm2. read more
  • Laser-guided bomb A bomb that steers toward a spot of laser light placed on a target; first combat-evaluated by the US Air Force in 1968. read more
  • Laser-phosphor projector A video projector in which blue laser diodes light a spinning phosphor wheel and an imaging chip forms the picture; no beam is scanned. read more
  • Laserist The person who performs or operates a laser show; in the analog era, the player of the console. read more
  • Laserium The laser show Ivan Dryer opened at Griffith Observatory in Los Angeles on November 19, 1973, played live to recorded music; it ran there until January 2002. read more
  • Laserium Light Synthesizer (LLS) Laserium's analog performance console, with more than 150 controls for image size, motor speeds, brightness, shape and color, by one former laserist's count. read more
  • Laserock Laserium's first all-rock program, from 1977, and the start of the rock-album planetarium format. read more
  • Laserschutzbeauftragter Germany's laser safety officer, appointed in writing before Class 3R, 3B or 4 lasers are operated by an employer who lacks the required expertise personally. read more
  • LASIK An excimer laser reshapes the cornea under a thin flap that is then laid back down. The word was coined in 1991. read more
  • Lasing Service slang for shining a laser at an aircraft or ship. read more
  • LaWS Laser Weapon System, the US Navy prototype that operated on USS Ponce in the Persian Gulf in 2014. read more
  • Letter of determination The FAA's reply to a notice of an outdoor laser operation, stating an objection or no objection. read more
  • Lidar Light detection and ranging: measuring distance by timing laser pulses. read more
  • Lidar speed detector A hand-held laser speed gun used in traffic enforcement, such as the LTI Marksman 20-20 a New Jersey court examined in 1998. read more
  • Liquid sky Haze or fog visibly swirling inside a flat sheet of scanned laser light. read more
  • LITT (laser interstitial thermal therapy) A laser fiber inside the body heats and destroys tissue while MRI tracks the temperature. read more
  • Longitudinal mode A wavelength whose round trip fits the cavity a whole number of times, and so can build up. read more
  • Low-level light therapy (LLLT) Low-power red or near-infrared light meant to stimulate tissue rather than cut it, as in hair-growth caps and combs; also called photobiomodulation. read more
  • LTPS Low-temperature polysilicon, the faster transistor material in AMOLED phone displays. read more
  • Lumia A soft, gauze-like moving pattern made by sending a beam through rippled glass or plastic. read more
  • Maser Microwave amplification by stimulated emission of radiation; the microwave forerunner of the laser, named in April 1954. read more
  • Masking plate Metal plates at the aperture that cut off any output below or outside a set line, whatever the software sends. read more
  • Maximum Permissible Exposure (MPE) The highest level of laser light a standard treats as acceptable for an eye or skin over a given time. For a visible beam and a quarter second it is about 2.5 mW/cm2. read more
  • MedWatch FDA's voluntary system for reporting problems with medical products. read more
  • Michelson interferometer An instrument that splits a laser beam down two arms and recombines it, so tiny changes in length show up as light. The core of LIGO. read more
  • Mode-locking Locking a cavity's modes in phase so they add into a train of picosecond or femtosecond pulses. read more
  • Nd:YAG capsulotomy Using a laser to open the clouded lens capsule after cataract surgery. read more
  • Nominal Ocular Hazard Distance (NOHD) The distance at which a direct beam has spread enough for its irradiance to fall to the MPE. read more
  • NOTAM A notice to aircrews. The UK CAA normally issues one for a notified outdoor laser display. read more
  • Numerical aperture How wide a cone of light a lens gathers. A higher value focuses a smaller spot; Blu-ray uses 0.85. read more
  • OPSL Optically pumped semiconductor laser: diodes pump a thin gain chip in a small cavity, giving a clean, narrow beam and chosen colors such as 577 nm yellow. read more
  • Optical lattice clock A clock that holds atoms in a grid of laser light and probes them with a clock laser at an optical frequency. read more
  • Optical maser Schawlow and Townes' term for the laser. read more
  • Optical molasses Crossed pairs of laser beams that slow atoms as if they were moving through syrup. read more
  • Optical tweezers A focused laser beam that holds tiny particles and living cells; the subject of half the 2018 Nobel Prize in Physics. read more
  • Output coupler The partly reflective mirror the beam leaves through. read more
  • PASS Professional Audience Safety System, the hardware used on projectors approved to scan audiences, used on selected units at Coachella 2025. read more
  • Paveway The US family of laser-guided bomb kits adopted after the BOLT-117 evaluation in the Vietnam War. read more
  • PCAOM Polychromatic acousto-optic modulator: a tellurium dioxide crystal driven by radio-frequency signals that set color and brightness on gas lasers. read more
  • Pepper's ghost A reflection illusion using a slanted transparent sheet; the method behind stage "holograms". read more
  • Peresvet Russian laser system announced by President Putin in 2018; its purpose has not been officially stated. read more
  • Performing rights organization (PRO) A body such as ASCAP, BMI, SESAC or Global Music Rights that licenses the public performance of songs to the business where the music is played. read more
  • Permanent blindness (Protocol IV) Irreversible, uncorrectable, seriously disabling loss of vision: acuity below 20/200 Snellen in both eyes. read more
  • Petawatt 10^15 watts of peak power, held for only femtoseconds. read more
  • Photocoagulation Heating tissue with light until it seals or dies; first used on the retina, with sunlight and arc lamps before lasers. read more
  • Photodiode A sensor that turns light into an electrical signal; scanners and disc players use them to read reflections. read more
  • Photodynamic therapy (PDT) A light-activated drug plus light of a matching wavelength kills the cells that absorbed the drug. read more
  • Photosensitizer The light-activated drug in photodynamic therapy, such as porfimer sodium. read more
  • Picosecond laser A laser with pulses in trillionths of a second, used for tattoos and pigmented lesions. read more
  • Pit A tiny pressed mark on a CD, DVD or Blu-ray that reflects light differently from the flat surface, encoding bits. read more
  • PMA (premarket approval) FDA's stricter review requiring clinical evidence of safety and effectiveness, used for LASIK and SMILE lasers. read more
  • Point The smallest thing laser software controls: an X position, a Y position, on or off, and usually a color. read more
  • Point (sample) One X-Y-color position sent to the projector. A frame is a list of them. read more
  • Population inversion The condition lasing needs: more atoms in the excited state than in the ground state. read more
  • Pre-pulse The weaker first laser shot that flattens a tin droplet before the main pulse turns it into plasma. read more
  • PRK (photorefractive keratectomy) Reshaping the surface of the cornea with an excimer laser, without cutting a flap. read more
  • Product Report Form FDA 3632, the manufacturer's report on a laser product to FDA. read more
  • Projection zone A named output area with its own size, position, geometric correction and safety settings. read more
  • Protocol IV The 1995 protocol to the Convention on Certain Conventional Weapons on blinding laser weapons, in force since 1998. read more
  • Pulsed dye laser A yellow-light laser that targets blood vessels, used for port-wine stains. read more
  • Pump The energy source that creates the population inversion, usually light or electricity. read more
  • Q-switched laser A laser making very short, high-peak-power pulses in nanoseconds, used to break up tattoo ink and pigment. read more
  • Q-switching Holding lasing back while gain builds, then releasing it as one intense pulse, typically nanoseconds long. read more
  • Raster An image built from closely spaced horizontal lines, as on a TV; too fast for scanner mirrors beyond low resolutions. read more
  • Real-time performance console A generally analog console that lets the laserist change size, position, color and effects live. read more
  • Red List The firms named in an FDA import alert whose products can be detained without examination. read more
  • Remote cutting Vaporizing thin sheet with a high-intensity beam and no assist gas. read more
  • Remote interlock connector The connector US law requires on Class IIIb and IV laser systems; an emergency stop plugs into it. read more
  • Resonant cavity Usually two facing mirrors that send light back and forth through the gain medium, turning an amplifier into an oscillator. read more
  • Ruby laser The first laser (1960): a pulsed red source at 694 nm. It flashes, so it was no use for drawing. read more
  • SAE AS6029 The standard an automated system must meet to replace human safety observers at an outdoor US show. read more
  • Safety observer A person watching the airspace or beam paths who can terminate the beam immediately; ILDA's draft standard calls the role a laser spotter. read more
  • Scan-fail A safeguard that blanks the beam if the scanners stop moving it. read more
  • Scan-failure safety system A system that cuts the beam if the scanning mirrors stop moving; listed by the UK HSE among standard controls for laser displays. read more
  • Scanning safeguard A feature that prevents exposure above the limits if the scanning mirrors slow or stop. Required on scanned laser products in the US by 21 CFR 1040.10(f)(9). read more
  • Security master control The 2013 FDA proposal's term for the control that would replace the key switch requirement. read more
  • Selective photothermolysis Choosing a wavelength and pulse length so heat stays in one target - pigment, blood or ink - and spares the tissue around it. read more
  • Show-day rate What a laser company charges per day of show, worked out from equipment write-off, overhead and margin. read more
  • Shutter A mechanical flag inside the projector that blocks the beam ahead of the scanners; the beam attenuator of the US rule. read more
  • SLT (selective laser trabeculoplasty) A laser treatment of the eye's drainage tissue that lowers eye pressure in glaucoma. read more
  • SMILE (small incision lenticule extraction) A femtosecond laser cuts a small disc inside the cornea, which the surgeon removes through a small incision. read more
  • Spontaneous emission An excited atom releasing light on its own, in a random direction. read more
  • Star operator The second person at a Laserium show, who followed a written script to time the house lights and the planetarium's star projector. read more
  • Stimulated emission A passing photon makes an excited atom release a second photon matching it in wavelength, direction and phase. read more
  • Strain The fractional change in length a gravitational wave causes; 10^-21 for the 2015 detection. read more
  • Target designator The laser that marks a target for a guided munition. read more
  • Teach track A track on Laserium's four-track show tape that helped new laserists learn the timing. read more
  • Time of flight Distance equals half the round-trip time of a light pulse multiplied by the speed of light. read more
  • Timecode A running clock signal, such as SMPTE or MIDI timecode, that keeps lasers, lights, video and audio on one timeline. read more
  • Track pitch The spacing between neighboring turns of a disc's spiral track, about 320 nm on Blu-ray. read more
  • TTL On/off-only laser control, giving seven colors and no fades. read more
  • UDI Unique Device Identifier; reusable, reprocessed US medical devices must carry it on the device itself. read more
  • Unenhanced vision The naked eye or eyes with ordinary corrective lenses, as Protocol IV uses the term. read more
  • Variable divergence A projector feature that lets the operator widen or narrow the beam during a show. read more
  • Variance FDA permission to vary from a requirement of a performance standard - for shows, the 5 mW cap in 21 CFR 1040.11(c) - on stated safety conditions. read more
  • Variance condition A requirement the FDA attaches to a variance approval, binding on the holder. The 3.0 m and 2.5 m audience separations are conditions. read more
  • VCSEL Vertical-cavity surface-emitting laser, a tiny chip laser that emits from its surface, used in phone depth sensors. read more
  • Vector (in show software) A point with automatic sample placement: the software adds samples along long lines and at corners. read more
  • Vector graphics Images stored as points joined by lines and drawn as outlines. read more
  • WDM Wavelength division multiplexing: sending several closely spaced laser colors down one fiber to multiply capacity. read more
  • White-light laser A mixed argon/krypton ion laser that emits red, green and blue lines from one tube. read more
  • X-Y scanning Drawing with one beam by steering it horizontally and vertically with two mirrors, the way an oscilloscope draws. read more
  • Zoning Software limits that keep beams out of set areas such as the audience, crew positions, balconies and glass. read more

Where to go next

  • ILDA: Haze, fog and lasers - Short, plain account of why beams need particles in the air and how haze and fog differ.

  • The Conversation: Tupac's rise from the dead was sadly not holography - Clear explanation of what a hologram is and why the stage version is Pepper's ghost.

  • ILDA: Accredited Professional Lasershow Company - The accreditation requirements, fees and the list of accredited companies.
  • FDA: Laser Light Shows - The US variance requirements and forms on one page.

  • Smithsonian NMAH: Supermarket Scanner - The actual 1974 scanner, with how it worked.
  • TeleGeography: Do Submarine Cables Account For Over 99% of Intercontinental Data Traffic? - The clearest check on how much data rides on subsea fiber.
  • LLNL: Achieving Fusion Ignition - The official, dated list of every NIF ignition shot.
  • LIGO Caltech: LIGO Detected Gravitational Waves from Black Holes - The detection story from the lab, with links to educational material.

  • Protocol on Blinding Laser Weapons (Protocol IV) - The treaty itself: four short articles, readable in five minutes.

  • Nordic statement on audience scanning - The clearest regulator position against scanning audiences.

  • All ILDA Award winners - Every ILDA Award winner, with links to the yearly pages.
  • ILDA: Laser show history - The best index to the history, with links to primary accounts of Krebs, The Who's lasers and the early companies.
  • W. Patrick McCray: Lasers, Pot Smoke, and the 'Visual Art of the Future' - A historian of technology on Laserium, Garmire and Dryer, with the patent and the hardware.
  • Lowell Cross: Remembering David Tudor - First-person account of the 1969 Mills College show and Expo '70, with model numbers and dates.
  • IMERSA: Light Years, a preview of Ivan Dryer's book on Laserium - Dryer's own chronology, venue by venue and show by show.
  • Glenn Thomas: The Laserium Laserist - A former laserist's memoir of what the job was each night, console and breakdowns included.
  • William Bridges: Ion Lasers - The Early Years - The inventor's account of the argon and krypton lasers that made colored shows possible.
  • LaserFX: A Brief History of Laser Light Shows - A 1990s trade history, strong on the art precursors and the first automated shows.
  • Fortune: The Last Laser Show - A 2002 business-press account of Laserium's rise and its final weeks at Griffith.

  • ASML: Light and lasers - The clearest official explainer of DUV versus EUV and the tin-droplet source.
  • TWI: Laser cutting (Job Knowledge 52) - Neutral engineering explainer on cutting modes and assist gases from a research body.

  • Coherent: Excimer laser annealing - How phone display backplanes pass under an excimer line beam.

  • AIP Center for History of Physics: Masers and Lasers - A clear narrative from the history-of-physics archive, including the Gould and Maiman episodes.
  • Charles H. Townes: The First Laser - A Nobel laureate's first-hand account of the ruby laser and its rejected paper.
  • Physics World: And then there was light - Day-by-day account of Maiman's experiment and the Bell Labs credit dispute.
  • Photonics Spectra: Laser Pioneers timeline - The densest dated laser timeline, 1951 onward.

  • FDA - Import Alert 95-04 - The product types, violations and firms FDA holds at the border.
  • LasershowSafety.info (ILDA) - Laser show incidents and accidents - The fullest public list of audience-injury reports, each with its source.
  • Canada Gazette - SOR/2024-196 - A recent national rewrite on IEC 60825-1, with Canada's injury and labeling data.
  • Justia - USA v. Sergio Rodriguez (9th Cir. 2016) - How an appeals court reads the federal laser-at-aircraft statute.
  • ILDA: Laser projector specifications - The most even-handed explanation of power, divergence, kpps and modulation claims, with warnings about how specs get inflated.
  • LaserFX: How Laser Shows Work - Projectors - The best record of gas-era hardware: beam tables, colour boxes, shutters and bounce mirrors.
  • Sam's Laser FAQ: Argon/Krypton Ion Lasers - Engineer-level detail on ion tubes, power supplies, cooling and wavelengths.
  • ILDA Test Pattern, revision 002 (1995) - The primary source for the 12K and 30K scanner standard.
  • 21 CFR 1040.10 (Cornell LII) - The US regulation's own list of required laser product safety features.
  • Jeff Hecht: History of Gas Lasers, Part 1 - Dated, sourced history of the helium-neon and ion lasers.
  • Justin Perry: Scanning advances brighten and enhance laser light shows - Trade-press account of how galvanometers developed and what limits them.
  • Coherent: 150,000 OPSLs and Counting - The clearest primary description of how an OPSL works.
  • FDA: List of FDA-Approved Lasers for LASIK - Every approved LASIK laser with its PMA number and date, from the regulator.
  • FDA: What are the risks of LASIK? - The regulator's plain-language list of LASIK risks and red flags.
  • Palanker, Blumenkranz, Marmor: Fifty Years of Ophthalmic Laser Therapy (2011) - A compact, dated history of eye lasers from ruby to femtosecond.
  • IBM Research: You have Thanksgiving turkey to thank for LASIK (2021) - The inventors' institution tells the excimer discovery story.
  • National Cancer Institute: Photodynamic Therapy to Treat Cancer - What PDT treats, how it works and its limits, reviewed in 2025.
  • FDA: Laser Toys - How to Keep Kids Safe (2023) - FDA's consumer guidance on pointers, toys and power labels.
  • Khalifian et al.: Causes of Injury and Litigation in Cutaneous Laser Surgery, 2012-2020 - The most recent data on cosmetic laser injury claims and who was operating.
  • ILDA technical standards - The primary list of the Standard Projector, test pattern, IDTF and IDN documents with revision dates.
  • LaserFX: How Laser Shows Work - Graphics System - A plain-language explanation of points, frames and why lasers draw vectors. Old (1996-2008) but foundational.
  • Pangolin Wiki: BEYOND manual, Vectors and Points - Working documentation on points, anchors, blanking and flicker. A vendor manual, so it shows how one product does it.
  • Glenn Thomas: The Laserium Laserist - The most detailed first-hand account of what an analog laserist did each night.
  • Event Safety Alliance: Laser Show Safety in Practice - A laser safety officer's account of setup, alignment and zoning on a show day.
  • ILDA Awards 2026 rules - Category definitions that double as a map of the kinds of laser show that exist.
  • ILDA: Laser Light Show Safety Overview - One page on what the operator is expected to do before and during a show.
  • FDA: Laser Light Shows - The US regulator's plain summary of the rules, forms and notices.
  • FDA Form 3147 - The variance conditions in the regulator's wording: 3.0 m and 2.5 m separation, no audience scanning by default, FAA notice.
  • FAA Advisory Circular 70-1B - Outdoor shows, flight zones and the filing process.
  • ILDA: Audience scanning safety - The trade body's explanation of MPE and its recommended limits, clear about what is and is not law.
  • DGUV Information 203-036 (German) - The most detailed free regulator-side document on show lasers, with diagrams and checklists.
  • HSE: The safety of laser lighting displays - The UK position on one page, with the pointer to PLASA's guide.
  • ILDA: Camera sensor damage - Thresholds, cases and practical notes on lasers and cameras.
  • O'Hagan PhD thesis (1998) - The deepest free study of show laser risk and UK regulatory history.

Still unconfirmed

15 open questions

Know the answer? hello@thirdpolararch.com

  • What does the FDA require inside an audience scanning variance? The agency says such effects need specific approval and publishes no limits or hardware list.
  • How were the band-titled planetarium shows (Laser Floyd, Laser Zeppelin) authorized, and on what terms? Nothing published settles it.
  • Who produced the 1983 Stone Mountain laser show, and with what lasers?
  • What did a complete argon and krypton show system cost in its day, and what does a 30 W to 100 W festival projector cost now? Makers publish gear lists far more readily than prices.
  • What are day rates, insurance costs and the real size of the laser show business? No public figure holds up.
  • When exactly did blue and red diodes become cheap enough to push DPSS and krypton out of show projectors?
  • Which famous sky beams are xenon searchlights and which are lasers? The Luxor beam in Las Vegas is the usual example and needs a primary source.
  • Which countries outside the US, UK, Germany, Canada and Australia regulate laser shows directly? The fullest list is one the trade association calls unofficial.
  • Has any named production documented running lasers over OSC or MIDI show control?
  • What did investigators conclude about the lasers at the 2008 Aquamarine festival? No official technical finding has been published.
  • Why was ILDA founded in 1986? Its own history page does not say.
  • What did the courts actually hold in Gordon Gould's laser patent cases? The accounts give the outcome but not the holdings.
  • How many LASIK procedures are done now? The newest published counts are 2011 industry estimates.
  • Which national programs fly or field laser weapons beyond what governments have announced, and at what power?
  • Who supplies the lasers for the biggest festival stages - EDC's main stage, Tomorrowland, Creamfields - and how many projectors? No rig list is public.
